# Tasks

Owner = who is working on it (agent label or "maintainer"). Mark done with ✅ and a one-line result. Add follow-ups
as new rows rather than editing old ones. Open questions for the maintainer go in the last section, each with a
suggestion, pros/cons and the A/B that would settle it.

A per-slot transform in the shader is what is left. a second vec4u in the slot record; today uv is TEXCOORD_0 as is Issue [#52](https://github.com/pmndrs/pathtracer/issues/52). |
| P1.4c2 | Textures: bindless path (`chromium-experimental-sampling-resource-table`, Tier 3) behind the same slot API | agent:bindless | 🟨 built; GPU verdict open | **Audit 2026-11-26:** verdict on a real GPU: P1.4c2a, [#32](https://github.com/pmndrs/pathtracer/issues/32). 2026-11-19. `TextureOptions.binding: 'table'` (`TextureLayout.binding`, adapter `pt_tex_binding=table`): the bucket arrays go into one `GPUResourceTable` (view i at index i, sampler i at buckets + i), `rtTexSampleRecord` is one `getResource` line (no switch, no texture bindings, no bucket merge: `maxBuckets` unlimited), `rtModuleWgsl` puts the enable line first, the pipeline layout gets `usesResourceTable`, `PtMegakernel` sets the table on its pass; wavefront and interval tracer refuse it. Every other kernel byte-identical (327 files, `size-snapshot`). 6 unit tests (`texture-table.test.ts`). **Found:** Chrome 160 Canary on the M5 exposes the feature (caps tier 3) but Metal fails any COMPUTE pass with a table (V296); an explicit layout needs `usesResourceTable: true`; a 1-layer texture's default view is `2d` and `getResource<texture_2d_array>` then silently returns black. **`--page bindless-check`** on SwiftShader: coherent lookups bit-identical to arrays (and to the CPU twin, 7.7e-6); divergent ones wrong in 0.75 x (1 - 1/buckets) of lanes (lane 0's index, V297), a full-subgroup `subgroupMin` waterfall fixes that but not subgroups with exited lanes, so the textured megakernel reads -72% (tex-checker) / -25% (tex-mask) there. On the real M5 GPU through a fragment shader (`check=fragment`, table in `beginRenderPass({ resourceTable })`: Metal takes tables in render passes, not compute) divergent and holed lookups are correct, bit-identical to arrays: the SwiftShader breakage is SwiftShader's. Memory: 20 groups = 8 merged arrays 33.3 MiB vs table 21.8 MiB (-35%). Follow-ups: P1.4c2a (3060 verdict), P1.4c2b (wavefront), P1.4c2c (native sizes) |

Sanity: EmissiveStrengthTest cube G = 0.481/1.013/2.011/4.011/8.009 for strength 1/2/4/8/16 (x2 each step, both renderers, offset is the rough specular sky); thin-wall probe (uniform env L=1, 256 spp, `tw-probe` scratch) centre pixels follow the two-sheet closed form T2 = T^2/(1-(1-T)^2), T = (1-R)/(1+R), within -1.7..-3.6% (backdrop-sphere interreflection) and pbrt vs Mitsuba within 0.3%; IOR 1.0 invisible (-0.1/-0.2%). **IOR variant `IORTestGrid-notex-A`** (S0.25 spheres dropped, rendered without `--coverage`, pbrt 271 s / Mitsuba 238 s at 4096 spp): floor relMSE(Mitsuba, pbrt) 3.1e-4, relBias +0.0% (class A regions 4.7e-4 incl. sphere edges). Open: no per-pixel material id AOV in either exporter or renderer (the masks above are projected bboxes of each glTF material group): add one (pbrt `Film` AOV is not in the RGB film; a flat-colour material export variant is cheaper) so the viewer can split verdicts by class; pbrt sigma_a fit for the volume rows; gold needs a second adjudicator (analytic mirror/furnace) before it gates |

| P3.1 | Wavefront scheduler, slice 1: `PtWavefront` (`packages/pathtracer/src/wavefront/`) beside the megakernel (D11); same estimator, SoA path state, queues + GPU-written indirect args, one command buffer per sample, no readback | agent:wavefront | ✅ | **Correct**: wavefront vs megakernel per pixel on Tier 0 + sponza-white at 1024 spp and 14 feature scenes at 256 spp (textures, medium, thin, layered stochastic + albedo, fuzz, thin film, aniso, coated metal, mix): relMSE 0..8e-13, max per-pixel relative difference 7e-5 (ClearCoat; every other scene <= 7.4e-6), mean difference <= 6.8e-7, 0 non-finite; vs pbrt/Mitsuba identical to the megakernel's table (cornell 7.25e-5 / 4.82e-4, sponza 9.33e-3 / 9.50e-3 at 1024 spp, furnaces PASS, quad-light closed form PASS). `tex-mask` throws (alpha MASK not in slice 1). **Kernel bytes** (WGSL, per kernel, D26 budget 50 KB): plain camera 3.2 / intersect 12.4 / shade 29.6 / shadow 10.5 / args 1.1 KB (megakernel 40.5); env shade 32.3; tex 32.9; pbr-stochastic-coatedMetal 40.3 (megakernel 51.1); pbr-albedo-fuzz-thinFilm 44.1 (megakernel 54.9): every wavefront kernel stays under 50 KB where the megakernel does not. **Path state** 64 B (80 B with a medium or textures; budget 96), plus 48 B hit + 48 B shadow ray + 12 B queues per path (720p: 159 MB state/hit/shadow/queue + 15 MB accum). **Provisional timings** (M5 Pro, matrix.ts `--interleave`, rows valid, load 2.4-3.5 for Cornell/Sponza, 9-12 for env-sphere, 17 for quad-light; best effort, quiet pass pending): ms/spp megakernel -> wavefront: Cornell 512x512 maxDepth 32 13.90 -> 8.61 (wf/mk 0.62, 5 paired runs 0.616..0.621), sponza-white 1280x720 49.51 -> 38.73 (0.78, 3 runs 0.782..0.783), env-sphere 384x256 1.8-2.2 -> 2.9-3.0 (1.4-1.7, small film: launch bound), quad-light 0.126 -> 0.193 (1.5-1.6, load 17), furnace-dielectric maxDepth 1000 20 s -> 95 s per 1024 spp (4000 empty-iteration dispatches per sample). Check: `node bench/adapters/pt-wavefront-agreement.ts --a <mk report> --b <wf report>`; `node bench/adapters/matrix.ts --renderers pt-megakernel,pt-wavefront ...` **Quiet-pass 20261115-1714 (NOT clean: load 3.4-6.4, background Spotlight/fileproviderd, rows valid, 0 marked loaded; rerun still owed)**, wf/mk paired min..max over 5 rounds: Cornell 0.622..0.627, sponza-white 0.766..0.864 (noisy; mk 49.6-55.2 ms), env-sphere 1.57..1.59, quad-light 1.51..1.59, furnace-dielectric 5.2..6.7x (85-110 ms): the provisional numbers hold. `bench/results/quiet-pass/20261115-1714/REPORT.md`. |
| P3.2 | Wavefront refill: a slot whose path ends gets the pixel's next sample, so the queues stay full (setting `refill: 'inline' \| 'batch'`, `refillThreshold` 0.25, `layers` K slots per pixel; `finish()` drains the tail with a lagged 20 B readback, early-out) | agent:wavefront | ✅ (settings off by default, default undecided: quiet pass) | **Design**: path slot = `layer * N + pixel`, a slot runs the samples `layer, layer + K, ...` of ITS pixel one after the other (no two slots ever write one accumulator, so no float atomics; accum.w = samples started in the slot; sample s of pixel p is run exactly once, so every Owen-Sobol stream equals the megakernel's). Queues, counters and path state persist across `encode`s (iteration parity continues, 4 uniform records per encode: no params-ring limit on passes); dormant slot = live bit (bit 29 of the depth word) clear. `inline`: the shade thread starts the next sample at once (queue order of restarted primaries is random). `batch` (Cycles' rule): shade clears the live bit and counts the slot idle; the args kernel dispatches the CAMERA kernel over the whole film (indirect, zero groups unless live < 25% of slots or 0) which scans the slots in pixel order and restarts every idle one, so restarted primary rays stay coherent. `finish()`: chunks of 32..256 iterations, an 20 B counter copy per chunk mapped while the next chunk runs (one chunk of lag); stops when queues + idle are 0; over-run <= 2 chunks of zero-workgroup iterations. `encode(n)` asks `n x itersPerPass` iterations (learned by `finish`, first guess `min(maxDepth+1, 16)`). **Correct** (megakernel vs wavefront, per pixel): Tier 0 at 1024 spp for refill, refill L4 and batch: relMSE 0..8e-13, max per-pixel rel diff 1.3e-5, 0 non-finite; sponza-white 1024 spp (inline, batch) 1.3e-14, max 2.2e-6; 14 feature scenes at 256 spp (inline L2, batch L2) <= 1.6e-12, max 7e-5 (ClearCoat, as slice 1); every cell `wf_min_samples == wf_max_samples == spp`; AOV and counter images with batch bit-equal to the plain wavefront's; the CPU protocol simulation (`layout.test.ts`: K layers, inline and batch, growing target, partial encodes, chunked finish) sees every (pixel, sample) exactly once. Kernel text: megakernel byte-identical (`size-snapshot`), refill shade 31.2 KB (batch 30.4, slice 1 29.6), all variants < 50 KB. **Provisional timings** (M5 Pro, `matrix.ts --interleave --repeat 3`, rows `valid`, load 2.7-7, 0 foreign GPU processes, best effort, quiet pass pending), ms/spp min..max, ratio to the megakernel in (): env-sphere 384x256 mk 1.80-1.81 / slice 1 2.86-2.87 / inline 2.56 / batch 2.82-2.84 / inline L2 1.70 / inline L4 1.26-1.27 (0.70) / inline L16 0.92 (0.51) / batch L16 1.05 (0.58); quad-light 256x256 mk 0.128-0.132 / slice 1 0.195-0.198 / inline 0.217 / batch 0.233 / inline L2 0.179-0.185 (1.4, best) / batch L2 0.191; cornell 512 maxDepth 32 mk 13.89 / slice 1 8.56-8.58 (0.62) / inline 7.80-7.83 (0.56) / batch 8.90-9.01 (0.65) / inline L2 10.0 / L4 9.8; sponza-white 1280x720 mk 49.40-49.45 / slice 1 38.56-38.69 (0.78) / batch 39.27-39.32 (0.79) / batch L2 38.92-38.99 / inline 47.78-47.80 (0.97: restarted primaries are scrambled, see V87); furnace-dielectric maxDepth 1000 mk 16.14-16.15 / slice 1 87.3-87.8 (5.4x) / inline 4.75-4.77 (0.29) / batch 5.30-5.32 / inline L2 2.97-2.99 / inline L4 2.07-2.09 (0.13) (slice 1 pays ~22 us per empty dispatch, 4 per iteration, 4000 per sample). **Read**: refill kills the long-path cost (18-42x vs slice 1); `layers` fixes the small-film launch-bound case (env-sphere 0.51x of the megakernel at L16; layers hurt Cornell and Sponza: state traffic); `batch` = slice 1 speed on Sponza, `inline` is best on deep uniform paths (Cornell) but loses 25% on Sponza; quad-light (65k paths, trivial shading) stays 1.4x slower than the megakernel in every variant (state read/write per path-iteration, no layers setting helps past L2). Proposed default (needs the quiet pass, D-row after it): `batch` when paths >= ~500k, `inline` + `layers` ~ 1M / pixels when the film is small, megakernel for trivial scenes. Not supported with refill: `layers > 1` together with `aovs` / `counters` / `hooks` (they index per-pixel buffers by slot; throws), alpha MASK (as slice 1). Check: `node bench/adapters/matrix.ts --renderers pt-megakernel,pt-wf-refill,pt-wf-batch,pt-wf-refill-l4 --scenes ... --repeat 3 --interleave`, then `pt-wavefront-agreement.ts --a <mk> --b <wf>`; renderer ids `pt-wf-refill`, `pt-wf-batch`, `pt-wf-refill-l2/l4`, `pt-wf-batch-l2` (`--extra pt_layers=N,pt_refill_threshold=F` override) **Quiet-pass 20261115-1714 (NOT clean, load 3.4-6.4; rerun owed)**, ratio to megakernel: Cornell inline 0.562..0.566 / batch 0.646..0.655 / L2 0.73 / L4 0.72; sponza-white slice 1 0.77..0.86 / inline 0.94..1.09 / batch 0.77..0.93; furnace-dielectric inline 0.29..0.56 (one noisy round) / batch 0.32..0.34 / L2 0.18 / L4 0.13; env-sphere inline 1.39..1.41 / L2 0.93..0.96 / L4 0.69..0.73; quad-light inline 1.69..1.85, best L2 1.39..1.49 (all provisional numbers hold). Layers sweep (env-sphere, inline vs mk): L1 1.40, L2 0.94, L4 0.71, L8 0.57, L16 0.513, L32 0.501, L64 0.512 (plateau from L16). `refillThreshold` 0.1/0.25/0.5 on Sponza batch: 0.795 / 0.795 / 0.796, no effect (the setting is applied: config records it). `REPORT.md` in the same dir. |

| P3.6 | Visibility-buffer primaries: `integrator.primary: 'vbuffer'` on both schedulers (default `'trace'`, every kernel byte-identical): the engine rasterises instance + triangle + material + unorm16 barycentrics per pixel, the integrator resolves the primary hit from it (no camera ray traced) and continues as usual | agent:vbuffer | ✅ (timing provisional, QUIET-PASS) | **Design** (`integrator/vbuffer.ts`, format and rules in its header): `rgba32uint`, x = instance + 1 (0 = miss), y = triangle, z = material (the engine writes it: bvh keeps per-triangle materials in its tree, the scene copy has none), w = b1 | b2 << 16 (unorm16). `ptVbufferHit(px, ray) -> RtHit` rebuilds the hit from the scene copy's surface table (barycentric position, geometric normal = object-space cross product through the inverse-transpose normal matrix = bvh's authored front side, `frontFace = dot(n, dir) < 0`, `t` = distance to the position) so `rtSurface`, textures, materials, NEE etc. are the traced path's code. Megakernel: first loop iteration reads the texture, later ones `rtClosest` (a cutout texel at depth 0 therefore continues by tracing: alpha MASK is exact with no alpha-tested raster). Wavefront: the camera kernel takes the jitter from the params, the intersect kernel resolves paths with `depth == 0` from the texture (plain and MASK variant; it now binds the scene copy: walk 3 + scene 2 + 4 + 1 texture, <= 10 storage buffers); shade / shadow / args untouched. **One jitter owner**: the ENGINE owns the sub-pixel offset, one per frame, passed with `setVbufferJitter(x, y)` (pixels, [-0.5, 0.5], y down; rides in the unused `origin.w` / `right.w` lanes of `PtParams`, so no struct or default kernel changes). Camera-ray sampler pair 0 (`PT_PAIR_JITTER`) is not consumed; every later pair is hash-keyed, so `dimensions.ts` is unchanged and no dimension shifts (proof: a same-seed V-buffer render differs from the traced one only through the jitter, relMSE 0.07x of the noise floor on Cornell). Constraints: `layout.positions`, `encode(encoder, 1)` per frame (one V-buffer), wavefront `refill` / `layers` throw; counters count only traced rays. **Bench** (not library): `bench/adapters/shared/vbuffer-raster.ts` (one non-indexed draw of the scene copy input's triangles, flat ids, depth32float, the integrator's own camera and the frame's jitter), adapter setting `primary` (`pt_primary=vbuffer`, renderer ids `pt-mk-vbuffer` / `pt-wf-vbuffer` for `--interleave`), `--page vbuffer-check`. **Correctness** (`vbuffer-check`, jitter x 3, Apple M5 Pro, `--load-wait 0`): (1) rasteriser vs the closest hit of the same camera rays, equal triangle: Cornell 99.9991%, tex-mask 100%, foliage-mask 99.996%, MetalRoughSpheres 99.932%, sponza-white 99.928% of ALL pixels, 100% / 100% / 100% / 100% / 99.9985% of interior pixels (a pixel is an edge if a 4-neighbour's traced triangle differs); the rest are ties (a different triangle at the same point, <= 0.068%) and silhouette pixels (hard mismatches 0 / 0 / 0.002% / 0.0003% / 0.004%, zero in interiors); position error over equal-triangle pixels: Cornell max 2.7e-5 m, tex-mask 2.4e-4, MetalRoughSpheres 4.3e-3 (p99 1.0e-4), sponza-white 3.6e-3 (p99 2.4e-4, mean 2.5e-5): the unorm16 barycentrics shifting the point inside its triangle plane, so the position error scales with the triangle's size; normals <= 5e-7, material mismatches 0. (2) WGSL `ptVbufferHit` vs the traced hit through a V-buffer built from the traced hits: 0 miss / id / material / front-face mismatches on all 5 scenes, `t` rel. error <= 6.3e-5, barycentrics <= 7.6e-6 (half a unorm16 step), normal difference 0. (3) Images at 1024 spp, both schedulers, vbuffer (seed 2) vs traced (seed 0) against the noise floor traced (seed 1) vs traced (seed 0): relMSE ratio Cornell 1.002, MetalRoughSpheres 1.002, tex-mask 0.997, foliage-mask 1.004, sponza-white 1.00001 (floors 1.1e-4, 4.7e-4, 4.5e-5, 1.5e-4, 1.46e-2); relBias <= 4e-6 / 1.7e-5 / -5e-6 / 1.4e-5 and sponza-white -1.7e-4 (floor -9e-6; cause below); 0 non-finite pixels. Wavefront vs megakernel with V-buffer primaries, same frames: relMSE <= 5.6e-15 on all five. **Sponza**: 23% of its tie pixels (437 of 1875) are opposite-wound duplicate triangles where the traced hit and the rasteriser's first-drawn twin have opposite normals: that is the whole -1.7e-4 (437 px of 3 x 921,600 = 1.6e-4; V95). No other scene has the effect. **Kernel bytes**: megakernel `vbuffer` 41,994 B (plain traced 40,472), `tex-mask-vbuffer` 43,888 (traced 42,362); wavefront `vbuffer` camera 1,965 (traced 3,302: no sampler), intersect 14,660 (12,440: surface table + resolve), shade 29,602 / shadow 10,567 / args 1,139 unchanged; `tex-mask-vbuffer` camera 2,163, intersect 23,929 (22,299), shade 33,410, shadow 20,831. All 101 files of `node bench/size-snapshot.ts` taken before the first edit are byte-identical after it (12 new vbuffer files added). `pnpm test` 985 pass (10 of them new, `vbuffer.test.ts`), `pnpm typecheck`, eslint green. **Timing (PROVISIONAL, best effort on the shared M5 Pro: load 1.7-2.7, 0 foreign GPU processes, 3 interleaved rounds each agreeing within 0.1%; the vbuffer rows INCLUDE the bench rasteriser's pass, one non-instanced draw per sample; quiet-pass row P3.6 owns the real numbers)**: ms per sample traced -> vbuffer (paired ratio): Cornell 512x512 maxDepth 32 megakernel 13.89 -> 13.89 (1.00x), wavefront 8.57 -> 8.59 (1.00x: the primary is one ray in ~tens); Cornell maxDepth 2: megakernel 1.605 -> 1.529 (0.95x), wavefront 1.693 -> 1.694 (1.00x); sponza-white 1280x720 (maxDepth 8, RR 4): megakernel 49.50 -> 46.95 (0.95x), wavefront 38.71 -> 36.38 (0.94x); sponza-white maxDepth 2: megakernel 26.75 -> 24.07 (0.90x), wavefront 24.12 -> 21.71 (0.90x). tex-mask / MetalRoughSpheres not timed. So a V-buffer pays where the primary is a large share of the rays (shallow paths, big scenes) and costs nothing elsewhere, even with a naive raster in the timed region; the engine's own G-buffer pass would not be in the frame at all. **R9-prep note (not built)**: for the real-time mode the V-buffer is the cheap half of the G-buffer: (a) at 1-2 bounces the primary ray is a large share of the traced rays, and a texture read replaces it (the raster pass is the engine's, usually already paid); (b) the same V-buffer yields the denoiser / upscaler guides with no trace: viewZ and normal from `ptVbufferHit`, albedo from the material at the texel, MOTION from the barycentric position and the PREVIOUS frame's instance transform (the scene copy keeps only the current ones: the engine must pass last frame's `objectToWorld` or a per-instance delta), computed jitter-free (CLAUDE.md: motion is jitter-free) even though the raster is jittered; our AOV albedo/normal follow specular bounces (R4), so a mirror pixel still needs the path, the V-buffer guide is the first-hit variant; (c) the jitter the raster used is the one the upscaler (DLSS-style, `~/Developer/upscaler`) must be given, which is why the engine, not the integrator, owns it; (d) vbuffer vs trace is EXACT (not a biased preset), so R9 has nothing to judge here beyond the opposite-wound-duplicates caveat; the real-time presets that matter are the ones layered on top (reduced BRDFs, radiance cache). **Follow-ups**: a real engine pass (instanced, indexed, per-instance material) replacing the bench raster; motion-vector pass (b); an f32 barycentric variant if the unorm16 shift (up to 3.6 mm on Sponza's big triangles) ever shows in a texture lookup; prefer-front-facing two-pass raster for duplicate twins; per-pixel V-buffer + refill (one V-buffer per in-flight sample) if wavefront refill is ever wanted with it; V-buffer-seeded AOV resolve without a path **Quiet-pass 20261115-1714 (NOT clean, load 3.7-6.0; rerun owed)**, vbuffer/traced paired min..max (raster INCLUDED): default depth: Cornell mk 0.998..1.003 / wf 1.001..1.008, sponza mk 0.947..0.949 / wf 0.940..0.942, tex-mask mk 1.002..1.069 / wf 1.041..1.068 (slower: 0.3 ms film, the raster pass is a fixed ~0.02 ms); maxDepth 2: Cornell mk 0.933..0.973 / wf 0.984..1.011, sponza mk 0.899..0.900 / wf 0.898..0.902, tex-mask mk 0.937..1.070 / wf 1.010..1.074: provisional numbers hold. MetalRoughSpheres not in the pass script. `REPORT.md`. |
| P3.7 | Wavefront alpha MASK: the intersect kernel loops over cutout hits (the megakernel's skip step, no depth cost), the shadow kernel is `ptVisibleMasked`; the throw is gone | agent:wavefront | ✅ | **Design**: `ptwfIntersectMaskWgsl`: closest hit, then while the hit is a MASK material whose bilinear alpha x opacity (ray-cone LOD, as the megakernel) is below the cutoff: apply the medium attenuation of the skipped segment to `beta`, move the origin past the hit (`ptOffsetOrigin`), carry the cone width into the path state, trace again (path state stored back only if a skip happened). Shade therefore only ever sees a solid hit or a miss, so no iterations are added; a path over `PT_MASK_SKIPS` (64) is marked `hit.w = 1` and shade returns (path ends, slot restarts under refill). Intersect and (exact shadow policy) shadow bind the scene copy: walk 3 + scene 2 + 4 wavefront buffers = 9 <= 10 storage buffers. **Correct** (`pt-wavefront-agreement.ts`, megakernel vs wavefront per pixel, 256 spp, plain / `pt-wf-batch` / `pt-wf-refill` all equal): `tex-mask` relMSE 4.2e-13, max per-pixel relative difference 2.4e-6, mean difference <= 7.9e-7, 0 non-finite; `foliage-mask` (new, `bench/scenes/ab/foliage-mask.ts`: 36 overlapping cutout cards, maxDepth 4) 3.5e-13 / 3.0e-6 / <= 5.6e-7 / 0; the same for every alpha policy (below, plain and batch vs megakernel <= 4.4e-13). Exact vs Mitsuba `tex-mask` 4096 spp: relMSE 1.44e-4, relBias +0.16% (pbrt/Mitsuba floor 2.5e-4 class; V88). **Kernel bytes** (wavefront `tex-mask`): camera 3.5 / intersect 22.3 / shade 33.4 / shadow 20.8 / args 1.1 KB (textured without MASK: intersect 12.4, shadow 10.6); megakernel `tex-mask` 42,362 B unchanged. All pre-existing megakernel and wavefront kernel variants (81 files of `node bench/size-snapshot.ts`) byte-identical before/after. **Not supported**: `integrator.counters` with alpha MASK on the wavefront (the re-traces happen in intersect, which has no counter buffer: `ptwfUnsupported`). Skip count is per ray in the wavefront, per path in the megakernel (only visible past 64 skips). Check: `node bench/adapters/matrix.ts --renderers pt-megakernel,pt-wavefront --scenes tex-mask,foliage-mask` then `pt-wavefront-agreement.ts` **Quiet-pass 20261115-1714 (NOT clean; rerun owed)**: exact wavefront / megakernel `tex-mask` 1.242..1.259 (provisional 1.23), `foliage-mask` 0.869..0.878 (0.87): holds. `REPORT.md`. |

| P3.9 | Auto policy for the refill settings (`refill` mode and `layers` from film size, depth limit and scene), then the D-row: A/B across Tier 0 / sponza / Tier 1 scenes at 3 film sizes | agent:wavefront | ✅ (provisional, D32): `ptwfAutoPolicy` is the recommended entry point (doc, no factory) and takes `kernelBytes` (> 50,000 B megakernel -> plain wavefront); the quiet-pass rows confirm the constants | ms/spp (quiet pass) vs the best fixed setting per cell; the P3.2 table says: batch for big films, inline + layers (~1M slots) for small ones, megakernel for trivial shading Quiet-pass 20261115-1714 (not clean) supports the proposed policy: batch for big films (Sponza 0.77..0.93 mk), inline + layers 16-32 for small (env-sphere 0.50-0.51), megakernel for trivial/small shading (quad-light every variant 1.4-1.9x slower); inline alone is best only on deep uniform paths (Cornell 0.56). See `bench/results/quiet-pass/20261115-1714/REPORT.md`. **Code (P3.9)**: `ptwfAutoPolicy({ width, height, maxDepth, subgroups })` (`packages/pathtracer/src/wavefront/auto-policy.ts`, adapter setting `auto` / renderer id `pt-auto`): maxDepth <= 2 -> megakernel; >= 2^19 pixels -> plain wavefront; >= 2^18 -> inline refill, 1 layer; smaller -> inline refill + layers (power of two, slots in (1 M, 2 M], max 32); subgroup appends when available; sort none. Derived from the provisional ratios above and below (quad-light 1.6-2.4x on every wavefront flavour; sponza plain 0.73-0.80 vs inline 0.93-1.07; cornell inline 0.53-0.55 vs plain 0.59-0.61; env-sphere L1 inline 1.43 vs plain 1.61, L4 0.68-0.73). The D-row A/B across Tier 1 scenes at 3 film sizes is still open. |

Per pixel identical to the megakernel. Provisional timing: paired ratios from `--page wf-sched-ab` (one page, all variants interleaved ABBA, 5 rounds, correctness vs the first renderer in the SAME runs: max relative pixel difference <= 1e-5), M5 Pro, load 4-20 from other agents (rows recorded in `bench/results/wf-sched-ab.csv`; quiet pass pending). **Result** (ratio to megakernel; inline / ordered / batch / plain): sponza 0.93-1.07 / 0.85-0.98 / 0.77-0.83 / 0.76-0.80; cornell 0.53-0.55 / 0.67-0.69 / 0.63-0.66 / 0.59-0.61; foliage-mask 1.03-1.09 / 0.97-1.03 / 0.99-1.02 / 0.88-0.92; env-sphere L1 1.43 / 1.48-1.50 / 1.57-1.61 / 1.61; env-sphere L4 0.68-0.73 / 0.66-0.82; quad-light 1.8 / 1.9 / 1.9-2.0 / 1.6. So ordering recovers about half of the Sponza loss but plain and batch stay better there, and inline stays best on Cornell (the extra scan kernel is paid every iteration): `ordered` is not picked by the auto policy. Chunked `finish()` early-out for the plain scheduler is still open. |
| P3.11 | Scene copy packing: `sceneCopy.packing: 'split' \| 'packed'` (`uploadSceneCopy({ packing })`, `scenePacking` on `PtMegakernel` / `PtWavefront`, `RtModuleOptions.scene.packing`; renderer ids `pt-mk-packed` / `pt-wf-packed`, page arg `pt_packing`): the table and the data share ONE storage buffer (`rtScene: array<vec4u>` = [header vec4 (dataBase, tableBase, dataWords, totalVec4s) \| table \| data]), freeing a binding in every kernel (wavefront shade 8 -> 7 of 10, intersect + MASK 9 -> 8, hooks +1: megakernel 5, wavefront shade 3). bvh packs its own buffers in its own session | agent:pack | ✅ default flipped to `packed` (D32, provisional; `split` is the setting for the old kernel text and for a scene copy over the one-binding cap; the quiet-pass row confirms) | **Design**: the table keeps its words unchanged at vec4 1 (so every table offset is the old one plus a compile-time constant `1u`), the data starts at vec4 `dataBase` (read from the buffer's first vec4: one uniform-address load; a uniform would cost the binding this frees, an `override` would recompile per scene) and a data word is `rtScene[dataBase + (i >> 2)][i & 3]` (`rtSceneWord`). The generators are untouched: `rtPackSceneAccess` rewrites `rtSceneTable[e]` -> `rtScene[e + 1u]` and `rtSceneData[e]` -> `rtSceneWord(e)` on a finished source (nested brackets, weak operators parenthesised, idempotent), applied in `rtModuleWgsl` and in both integrators' builders for their appended text, so `split` is byte-identical (all 113 files of `node bench/size-snapshot.ts` before / after: `diff -r` empty; `... packed` writes the packed set, 40,414 B plain vs 40,472 split, 49,179 vs 49,237 pbr-albedo). Textures / samplers shift down one binding. `uploadSceneCopy` throws a RangeError naming table + data + the limit when the SUM is over the device's binding size (Sponza 21.0 MiB; a scene with ~1.5M unwelded triangles hits 128 MiB, only the table adds to split's data-only limit); `writeSceneCopy` rewrites a rebuilt copy in place for either packing (reflections' `setTransforms` / `setLights` pattern). **Correctness**: `--page scene-copy` (packing=both, now also raw table / data words and an in-place rewrite): 2,171,588 checks, 0 mismatches, every accessor bit-exact on both; `trace-core packing=packed` (edges, cornell, quad-light): 0 closest / visible / surface mismatches; `shade-check packing=packed` all twins pass; `vbuffer-check check=resolve packing=packed` 0; `kernel-compile packing=packed` compiles every variant; unit tests (`scene-packing.test.ts` x2). Images (64 spp, both schedulers, `matrix.ts`): cornell, quad-light, sponza-white, tex-checker, tex-mask, openpbr-layered-furnace bit-equal to split; IORTestGrid (both schedulers) and env-sphere (wavefront) differ by <= 3.2e-7 relative (1-3 ulp, relMSE 3e-17, 0.4-4% of values); split is deterministic run to run and a text-only change (`pt_shorten=0`) stays bit-equal, so it is the packed kernel's different code, not an address error (V100). **Hook budget** (`pt-outputs-check check=budget`, M5 Pro): split 4 / 3 (megakernel, with counters) and 2 / 1 (wavefront shade), packed 5 / 4 and 3 / 2. **Timing (PROVISIONAL, load 3.6-6.2, 0 rounds marked loaded, 5 interleaved rounds)**: packed / split ms per sample: cornell 512x512 256 spp megakernel 1.017..1.021 (14.17 vs 13.89 ms), wavefront 1.004..1.024; sponza-white 1280x720 64 spp megakernel 1.002..1.003, wavefront 0.983..1.001: a wash on the big scene, ~2% slower on a tiny one (the data accessor is a shift, a mask and a dynamic lane read on top of one extra header load per data read). **Suggestion**: default `packed` if the quiet pass keeps it within ~2%, because it frees a binding everywhere. Follow-ups: hoist the `dataBase` load once per `rtSurface`; read the three vertex floats as one or two vec4 loads (`rtVertex3`); the hooks / reflections `resources()` need `copyBuffers.packed`. |

Refused with a message (`ptRestirUnsupported`): stochastic layering, fuzz, thin film, anisotropy, `materialLodDepth` 0, hooks (V173). **Checks** (`restir-check unify`, M5 Pro, 256 accumulated frames vs 1024-frame NEE, relBias in %): M = 1 equivalence vs PCG NEE relMSE 1.25x tex, 1.27x pbr, 1.09x mask (ris1 relBias <= 0.37%); unbiased M = 8 + temporal + k = 2 relBias plain / sharedHit / blue noise / V-buffer: tex -0.08 (+0.12 seed 2) / -0.08 / -0.04 / -0.07, mask +0.08 (-0.01) / +0.08 / -0.04 / +0.06, **pbr -0.66 (-0.45) / -0.66 / -0.32 / -0.35**; relMSE vs NEE 0.65x tex, 0.67x pbr, 0.35x mask; no non-finite pixel. **Bias FIXED (bias hunt, see the CLAUDE.md traps)**: not the OpenPBR adapter, `ptEval` or the stored pick (pick shaded = pick stored). Single-seed relBias of an accumulated run is noise of ~1e-3 (the "Lambert -0.13%" was noise); `restir-check biasscan` (24-32 seeds, mean +- SE, `restir-pbr-lamps-*` variants) located it: only the stochastic `mix` material was off (`-mixonly` -0.23% of the image, -15% on the box top), only with temporal + spatial together, only when the pick changes between frames (`rnd` / `frm` picks: yes; fixed per pixel or constant: no). Cause: the temporal and spatial merges shared one PCG key base (and the temporal sub-pixel shift key 7 collided with candidate 3's u), so the spatial replace decision was correlated with the temporal one and the kept sample was not chosen proportionally to its weight. Fix: one key per random draw (`PT_RESTIR_KEY`). **After** (unbiased M = 8 + temporal + k = 2, 512 frames, 24 seeds, megakernel, relBias in 1e-4 vs the NEE row of the same run, mean +- SE): restir-pbr-lamps +0.9 +- 3.4 (NEE -0.9; was -36 +- 3.4 at 256 frames), -nomix +2.8 +- 3.5, -gold +7.2 +- 3.0 (NEE +2.7), -lambert +4.1 +- 2.2, -mixonly +3.1 +- 2.0 (was -23 +- 2.5), many-lights-64 +4.8 +- 2.4 (was +11 +- 2.2), lights-mix-lamps +2.8 +- 0.9; every scene within 4.5e-4 of the NEE row (the largest: gold +4.5 +- 3.0 and lights-mix-lamps +2.6 +- 0.9 above it, both under the 3e-4 .. 5e-4 seed-spread bar; a residual positive offset of ~2e-4 on the legacy scenes is left unexplained). Spatial only and temporal only were never biased. ReSTIR kernels: only the ten `restir-passes*` kernels change (text), every shade / megakernel / wavefront kernel is byte-identical |

the megakernel's frame surface (`setCamera` / `nextFrame` / `setPrevCamera` / `setFrameIndex`, `encode(e, 1)`, `readFrame()`, `frameBuffers`, `accumulator`); `frameBuffers` = `{ accum, planes (the same buffer), planesOffset, width, height }`; the previous camera is a dynamic-offset uniform (binding 13), the blue-noise texture binding 12; depth-0 shade writes planes 2-5 + the emission, everything after is added pre-divided by the primary class's factor (`ptFrameAdd`: shade and shadow kernels); `frameEmission: 'mirror'` keeps one `impure` bit in the path state (V211). **Checks** (M5 Pro, `--load-wait 0`): `--page wf-frame-check` runs both kernels lock-step 6 frames (orbit / pan) and compares all 8 planes per pixel: Cornell 128, tex-checker, restir-pbr-lamps (OpenPBR, albedo layering, stochastic mix), restir-tex-lamps, sponza-white 1280x720: max relative difference (vec4 norm) 1.6e-7 .. 5.0e-7 (gate 1e-5), geometry / motion / factor / emission planes BIT-identical, 82-99% of the beauty pixels bit-identical, `diffuse x albedo + specular x specular factor + emission = radiance` to 2.4e-7; all under 50 KB. Off by default: all 187 pre-existing `size-snapshot` variants (split and packed) byte-identical, and the megakernel frame text equal to main's. **Open**: ~~an alpha-MASK scene with `mode: 'realtime'` differs from the megakernel per pixel~~ fixed in P4.1c (the intersect kernel's cutout test lacked the R1 footprint widening); first-cutout bounce distance in plane 0 / 1 alpha differs by design (V211); a path that overruns the 64 cutout skips leaves stale planes (rare). |
| P4.1c | Wavefront alpha MASK x R1 x cone LOD per-pixel bug (found by P4.1b: `foliage-mask`, `mode: 'realtime'`, up to 32% in ~17% of pixels) | agent:wavefront | ✅ fixed, checked per pixel | **Root cause**: the cutout decision of the wavefront lives in the INTERSECT kernel, which is built with `materialLod: 'full'` (R1: only the shade kernels know the lod), so its texture read for the cutout test used the plain cone footprint `fp`. The megakernel reads the material ONCE per hit (`mat = rtApplyMaterialTextures(.., fp * select(1, PT_LOD_TEX_BIAS, depth >= PT_LOD_DEPTH))`) and decides the cutout from that same texel. At depth >= `materialLodDepth` the two therefore skipped different texels (a card edge alpha near the cutoff passes at the fine footprint and fails at the 2x one, or reverse): different path geometry afterwards, not a cone-update difference (the cone width, origin and medium `beta` the intersect kernel writes back were right). Needs all three: `textureLod` 0 (`fp` 0) or `materialLod: 'full'` (bias 1) alone make the footprints equal. **Which is right**: the megakernel: R1 widens the footprint of every texture read of a reduced vertex, and the cutout is a read at that vertex; the wavefront was also inconsistent with itself (intersect passed a hit that its own shade kernel, reading the widened footprint, would have called transparent, but shade never re-tests a solid hit). **Fix**: `PtwfConfig.lodTexDepth` (set in `ptWavefrontBuild` only for `materialLod: 'secondary'` + textures + cone + alpha MASK) adds `* select(1.0, 2.0, st.depth >= N)` to the intersect kernel's `fp`; every other kernel and every other option set is byte-identical. **Checks**: `wf-frame-check cases=foliage,foliage-lod2,foliage-tex0,foliage-matfull,foliage-plain,tex-mask` (new `foliage-lod2`: 4 bounces, threshold 2; `tex-mask`; radiance plane before: foliage 0.38 / foliage-lod2 0.30 / tex-mask 0.35 max relative, 11126 / 889 / 325 pixels over the 1e-5 gate; after: <= 1.2e-7, 0 pixels), the diffuse plane compared on rgb only for MASK cases (its alpha is the first bounce distance, which differs by design, V211); `matrix.ts` + `pt-wavefront-agreement` megakernel vs wavefront, 64 spp, maxDepth 4, `pt_texture_lod=cone`, foliage-mask and tex-mask, refill off / inline / batch / ordered, `pt_lod=secondary` depth 1 and 2 and `full`: max relative pixel difference <= 1.12e-6, mean <= 2.5e-7, 0.000% pixels over 1e-3 (before, `secondary` depth 1: foliage-mask mean +1.2..2.7%, 52.7% of pixels over 1e-3, tex-mask 9.5%); `lod-wgsl.test.ts` text test. `node bench/size-snapshot.ts split`: all 219 existing variants byte-identical; the new variant `wf-tex-mask-cone-lod-*` (`kernel-variants.ts`) differs from the unfixed text in the intersect kernel only (+32 B). **Open**: the 64-skip overrun case in frame planes (P4.1b) is unchanged. |

## P5 — ReSTIR GI (first item of the Breadth phase; `docs/internal/design/restir-gi.md`)

## Real-time plan additions (from research note 12, NVIDIA PT performance whitepaper)

Proposed 2026-11-15; each is a setting decided by A/B, never on in reference mode unless unbiased.

| ID | Task | Phase | Status | A/B that settles it |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | Ray-class material LOD: `integrator.materialLod: 'full' (default) \| 'secondary'`, `materialLodDepth` N (default 1); vertices at depth >= N evaluate a reduced OpenPBR material (BIASED, never in reference mode; page args `pt_lod`, `pt_lod_depth`, renderer ids `pt-mk-lod`, `pt-wf-lod`) | P3 | ✅ (default stays `full`; quiet pass pending for timing) | **Reduction** (`ptBsdfReduced`, `bsdf/adapter.ts`, TS twin + WGSL, V89; design in integrator.md "Ray-class material LOD"): a layered / coated dielectric becomes ONE random lobe per vertex (GGX with Fresnel 1 with probability q = s E(mu_o) / A, else Lambert D / (1 - q), the albedo layering's own albedo, internal base), a coated metal a plain conductor fitted at the view angle, fuzz folded in (Lambert lobe + base weight 1 - w E_f from a 9x9 table), anisotropy -> mean alpha, thin film dropped, cone footprint x2. **Kernel**: megakernel = run-time branch (grows 2.7-8.5 KB); wavefront (plain scheduler) = default `shade` byte for byte for depth < N + a second `shadeSecondary` kernel from depth N (V90): 37.3-45.5 KB vs 39.7-54.8 KB shade (0.81-0.94x; `node bench/lod-sizes.ts`). **Byte identity**: 'full' leaves all 113 `size-snapshot` variants identical to main. **Furnace** (`--page lod-check`, sphere under a unit sky, every vertex reduced (depth 0), 512 spp, mean over the sphere reduced vs full albedo layering): dielectric over diffuse grey r0.1/0.3/0.6, red, dark +0.1..+0.4%; coat r0.05 / 0.3 -0.3..-0.8%; coated gold -1.0..0%; fuzz over grey +0.2%, fuzz over layered +0.3..+1.5%; film (dropped) -1.8..-0.5%; metal mix 0.3 +0.1%; per-view-angle bins <= 4% (except coated-gold-rough: -7% blue, one bin 35% at grazing, see R1c). Against the stochastic walk (reference mode) the same numbers are the D24 gap, not the reduction's: grey r0.6 +41%, r0.3 +5% (P2.32b). Wavefront = megakernel per pixel (reduced): relMSE <= 1.8e-13 on 11 scenes, AnisotropyStrengthTest 7.6e-9 (one pixel, a lobe-pick threshold tie). Unit tests: `bsdf/lod.test.ts` (albedo within 3% + 0.005 over roughness/base/mu, coat, coated metal 6%, fuzz 0.012 abs), `wavefront/lod-wgsl.test.ts`. **A/B** (albedo layering kernel, 4096 spp, lod depth 1 vs full; relBias whole image / 32 px block max / relMSE lod(1024) vs full(4096) over full(1024) vs full(4096)): ClearCoatTest-notex -0.00% / +0.60% / 3.6e-5 vs 1.7e-5; ClearCoatTest-notex-metal -0.01% / +1.36% / 1.10e-4 vs 8.9e-5; MetalRoughSpheresNoTextures (-grey-metal, textured) 0.00% / +0.08% (0.00) / 1.06e-4 vs 9.9e-5; SheenTestGrid-notex -0.01% / +1.07% / 5.4e-5 vs 3.9e-5; SheenTestGrid -0.01% / +1.75%; IORTestGrid-notex-A and -A -0.00% / +0.1% / 4.3e-4 vs 3.9e-4; AnisotropyStrengthTest-notex +0.08% / +9.84% / 1.15e-3 vs 3.4e-4; sponza-white and cornell are legacy-adapter scenes (no OpenPBR material): identical. vs pbrt / Mitsuba (class A) full and lod agree to 3 digits (IORTestGrid-notex-A 2.53e-4 vs 2.61e-4 against pbrt, floor 3.1e-4). Stress scene `lod-cornell` (Cornell, rough layered walls + coated red + fuzzy green + coated rough gold block, maxDepth 8, 8192 spp): depth 1 +0.07% mean, block max +14% in a dark corner (mean 0.024) / rms 2.6%, relMSE 7.3e-4 vs 4.5e-4; depth 2: -0.01% / 2.1% / 0.5%; depth 0 (every vertex) +1.2% / 43% / 12%; for scale, full-albedo vs the stochastic walk is -2.0% / 27% / 8.5%. **Provisional ms/spp** (M5 Pro, `matrix.ts --interleave --repeat 5`, 512 spp, load 1.9-2.4, 0 foreign GPU processes, rows valid, best effort), lod / full paired ratio, megakernel: IORTestGrid-notex-A 0.902-0.905, lod-cornell 0.916-0.920, ClearCoatTest-notex 0.955-0.960, SheenTestGrid-notex 0.976-0.978, AnisotropyStrengthTest-notex 1.003-1.007, MetalRoughSpheresNoTextures 1.010-1.013, MetalRoughSpheres 1.011-1.019, sponza-white 1.000-1.001 (no-op). Wavefront (plain), after the crash restart, 512 spp x 3 rounds, load 4-9 from other agents (rows not flagged `loaded`, but noisy: provisional): IORTestGrid-notex-A 0.930-0.946, ClearCoatTest-notex 0.985-0.987, lod-cornell 0.997-1.009, SheenTestGrid-notex 1.045-1.098 (spread 20.0-21.4 ms): the wavefront gains little, shade is not its bottleneck; the win is kernel size, not time.  **R1b DONE:** `materialLod: 'secondary'` works with every refill mode (V113: both shade kernels gate on the path depth, the first marks consumed entries; lod-cornell vs `pt-mk-lod` max rel pixel 1.5e-6 for plain, inline and ordered refill; provisional ms/spp lod-cornell: mk-lod 2.87-2.94, wf-lod 3.59-3.68 (1.25-1.26x), inline+lod 5.23-5.25 (1.79-1.83x), ordered+lod 4.80-4.84 (1.63-1.69x), load 5: the wavefront does not pay on this small-kernel scene, lod or not). **Open:** R1c coated rough metal at grazing (-7% blue, the stack uses E(mu_o) twice where the albedo path integrates keep(wi)); R1d the reduced material models the albedo layering, so against the walk it inherits P2.32b; R1e depth >= 2 as a default? (depth 2 halves the block bias on lod-cornell, depth 1 saves more); R1f timing of the megakernel branch with the stochastic walk compiled (the reduction replaces the walk: expected larger win) and DXC compile time of `shadeSecondary` (W10) | full vs reduced secondary on Tier 1 + stress scene (done above); quiet pass for ms/spp |
| R2 | Per-ray-class alpha policy `integrator.alphaPolicy: exact \| opaqueShadow \| opaqueIndirect \| opaqueSecondary` (BIASED unless `exact`; both schedulers; page arg `pt_alpha_policy`, renderer ids `pt-mk-{shadow,indirect,secondary}`, `pt-wf-*`) | P3 | ✅ (default `exact`; the opaque ones are not recommended, see read) | **Policies**: `opaqueShadow`: shadow rays are plain any-hit walks (`rtVisible`), no closest-hit loop, no texture fetch; `opaqueIndirect`: extension rays after the first scattering vertex (depth >= 1) stop at a cutout card (camera rays and shadow rays stay exact); `opaqueSecondary` = both. **Quality** (1024 spp vs our exact 16384 spp render, `node bench/adapters/alpha-policy-ab.ts --report p37-ab --ref ...`, mean bias = relBias, relMSE): `tex-mask` exact 2.1e-5 / 0.00% (vs Mitsuba 1.4e-4 / +0.16%), opaqueShadow 3.0e-2 / -5.4%, opaqueIndirect 8.5e-3 / -2.9%, opaqueSecondary 5.4e-2 / -8.4%; `foliage-mask` exact 6.9e-5 / 0.00%, opaqueShadow 6.0e-2 / -12.7%, opaqueIndirect 9.3e-3 / -3.8%, opaqueSecondary 9.5e-2 / -17.3% (error = bias: a card casts the shadow of its whole square, bounce rays stop at holes). **Provisional timing** (M5 Pro, `matrix.ts --interleave --repeat 5`, rows `valid`, load 3.2-5.4, 0 foreign GPU processes, best effort; quiet pass pending), ms/spp ratio to the exact policy of the same scheduler, paired min..max: megakernel `tex-mask` shadow 0.862-0.870, indirect 1.030-1.050 (slower), secondary 0.874-0.886; `foliage-mask` shadow 0.791-0.810, indirect 1.004-1.013, secondary 0.768-0.775 (exact 1.27-1.28 ms/spp); wavefront `tex-mask` shadow 0.940-0.951, indirect 0.988-1.006, secondary 0.927-0.943; `foliage-mask` shadow 0.880-0.886, indirect 0.956-0.982, secondary 0.821-0.827 (exact 1.11 ms/spp). **Read**: the cheap walk buys 8-23% on the shadow side and nothing on the indirect side (the megakernel already computes the texture lookup for shading, so passing a hole costs only the extra trace, and few bounce rays hit holes), against 3-17% too dark: no policy is worth it for a quality renderer; the setting exists as an A/B and for a future preview mode. Kernel text: `tex-mask` megakernel exact 42,362 B (unchanged), opaqueShadow 44,316 (the any-hit walk is now compiled in; exact prunes `bvhTraceAny`), opaqueIndirect 42,377, opaqueSecondary 44,331. Open: no foliage Mitsuba/pbrt reference (own exact render used; `foliage-mask` is a registered scene, `render.ts --scenes foliage-mask --renderers mitsuba` would make one); `opaqueCache` (the original idea: cutouts opaque only for rays a cache will consume) needs a cache consumer (gi) **Quiet-pass 20261115-1714 (NOT clean, load 3.8-5.9; rerun owed)**: ratios to the same scheduler's exact policy hold (mk tex-mask shadow 0.845..0.868 / indirect 1.025..1.043 / secondary 0.874..0.881; foliage-mask 0.793..0.804 / 1.008..1.020 / 0.770..0.780; wf tex-mask 0.939..0.955 / 0.979..1.003 / 0.933..0.947; foliage-mask 0.880..0.886 / 0.953..0.958 / 0.818..0.822), bias vs our 16384 spp exact renders unchanged (alpha-policy-ab.ts). **How many ms is the 8-23% (median ms saved per sample at 320x240, M5 Pro, vs the same scheduler's exact; 1080p = x27 pixels, an ESTIMATE, linear in pixels)**: megakernel foliage-mask exact 1.265 ms: shadow -0.260 ms (20.6%, 7.0 ms at 1080p) for 12.7% darker, secondary -0.284 ms (22.5%, 7.7 ms) for 17.3% darker, indirect +0.019 ms (slower) for 3.8% darker; megakernel tex-mask exact 0.324 ms: shadow -0.046 ms (14.1%, 1.2 ms) for 5.3% darker, secondary -0.039 ms (12.2%, 1.1 ms) for 8.4%, indirect +0.012 ms for 2.9%; wavefront foliage-mask exact 1.104 ms: shadow -0.131 ms (11.9%, 3.5 ms) for 12.7%, secondary -0.198 ms (17.9%, 5.3 ms) for 17.3%, indirect -0.048 ms (4.4%, 1.3 ms) for 3.8%; wavefront tex-mask exact 0.406 ms: shadow -0.021 ms (5.3%, 0.6 ms) for 5.3%, secondary -0.023 ms (5.6%, 0.6 ms) for 8.4%, indirect -0.005 ms (1.3%) for 2.9%. So the saving is 5-23% of the path-trace time but it is 0.02-0.3 ms per sample at 320x240 (0.6-7.7 ms at 1080p, per sample per pixel): never worth the darkening (megakernel secondary: 22.5% faster, 17.3% darker). Full table in `REPORT.md` / docs/internal/QUIET-PASS.md. NB `alpha-policy-ab.ts` reads the LAST 5 interleaved CSV rows of a cell, which after the later p36 runs belong to p36 for tex-mask exact (its tex-mask timing columns above are wrong in this pass); use `paired-ratios.ts --report p37-ab-20261115-1714 --base-map ...` (per-round run.json) for timing. |
| R2b | Recover the brightness lost by opaque cutouts at (almost) the opaque cost (D27): A/B of cutout shadow modes `integrator.alphaShadow` (`roulette`, `coverage`, `stochastic`, `firstK`, `transmit`, `classified`; both schedulers; page `alpha-shadow-ab`, report `bench/results/r2b/REPORT.md`, per-round data `bench/results/r2b/*.json`) | P4 | ✅ built, merged onto track N main (2026-11-18) and measured on every scene (provisional timings, load 3-9); preset change = D31 (`preview` / `realtime`: exact + `transmit` 0.45; max speed `alphaPolicy: 'opaqueShadow'` opt-in); quiet pass still to confirm (QUIET-PASS R2b) | **Result** (megakernel / wavefront ratio of best rounds to the exact policy, solid = `opaqueShadow`; relBias vs our exact 16384 spp render): `foliage-mask` solid 0.76 / 0.89 (-12.7%); `transmit@0.455` 0.79 / 0.92 (0.00%, relMSE 1.0e-1: hole pattern blurred), `transmit@0.45` 0.81 / 0.92 (-0.14%), coverage 0.76 / 0.89 (+1.3%), roulette(q .25) 0.88 / 1.01 (0.00%, noise x8, time to equal noise x7), roulette(q .1) 0.84 / 0.98 (noise x22), stochastic 0.87 / 0.99 (-0.4%), firstK 0.90 / 0.98 (-1.7%), classified 0.98 / 1.02 (0.00%, all triangles mixed). `foliage-mask-dense` (megakernel): solid 0.70 (-8.8%), `transmit@0.323` 0.79 (+0.01%), `transmit@0.45` 0.81 (+3.5%), coverage 0.80 (+4.9%), roulette(.1) 0.84, classified 1.00. `tex-mask`: solid 0.87 / 0.95 (-5.35%), `transmit@0.459` 1.02 / 0.97 (0.00%; vs Mitsuba the same as exact), classified = exact 1.15 / 0.99. `foliage-mask-grid16` (cut-out geometry, 0% mixed triangles): classified 0.515 wavefront (0.53 megakernel at a quiet single run: 1.36 vs 2.57 ms/spp) at exact quality (-0.01%), faster than solid 0.87; `grid12` (56% mixed) classified 1.10-1.15x SLOWER than exact. Triangles mixed / solid / transparent (`node bench/scenes/alpha-classify-stats.ts`): tex-mask 2/0/0, foliage-mask 72/0/0, dense 200/0/0, grid16 0/9216/9216 (of 18432), grid12 5760/2304/2304 (of 10368). **Target (under 3% darker for under 10% of the opaque saving lost): met by NO mode on plain 2-triangle cards at the measured noise (+-3% on the best-round ratios)**; closest are `transmit` calibrated (0% bias, 10-32% of the saving lost, pattern blurred) and `coverage` (+1.3..4.9% bright, 7-34% lost). On cut-out geometry `classified` meets it by a wide margin (exact, faster than exact). `roulette` is unbiased but 5-30x worse at equal noise: rejected. **Recommendation** (to confirm on a quiet pass): realtime / preview `alphaPolicy: 'exact'` + `alphaShadow: 'transmit'` (name for P4.1; beta 0.45, or calibrated from the two probes), NOT `opaqueSecondary` (its indirect half buys nothing, V147); `alphaShadow: 'classified'` for any scene whose cutout cards are authored as cut-out geometry (check `alpha-classify-stats.ts`: mixed share < ~30% was not measured, 56% loses). Reference mode stays `exact`. **Final pages done 2026-11-18** (`bench/results/r2b/REPORT.md`, section "final, grid16 and grid12"; load1 3.4-8.8, 7 rounds, megakernel / wavefront best-round ratios to exact): `foliage-mask-grid16` solid 0.79 / 0.87, `transmit@0.446` 0.85 / 0.91 (-0.01%; 26-28% of the solid saving given back), classified 0.52 / 0.55 (exact); `foliage-mask-grid12` solid 0.79 / 0.86, transmit@0.446 0.85 / 0.87 (26% / 7% lost), classified 1.16 / 1.08 (56% mixed: slower than exact). **D31** adopts the recommendation: `preview` / `realtime` = `alphaPolicy: 'exact'` + `alphaShadow: 'transmit'`, `alphaTransmit` 0.45; `alphaPolicy: 'opaqueShadow'` is the documented max-speed opt-in (`PT_MODE_NOTES`, `docs/internal/design/realtime.md`). The R2b merge onto main (track N) kept main's side everywhere; every existing kernel is byte-identical with the new settings off (133 size-snapshot files) and megakernel vs wavefront agree per pixel (<= 3e-6) for `transmit` / `classified`. Smoke of the preset (`--extra pt_mode=preview`, foliage-mask 256 spp, mean R): preview 0.3437 (transmit tagging worked; preview also has reduced materials and depth 4), `pt-mk-transmit@0.45` 0.3506, `pt_mode=preview pt_alpha_policy=opaqueShadow` 0.2972 = `pt-mk-shadow` 0.2972 (the solid walk). | X-trace-core | `trace-core scenes=cornell,quad-light sceneTrees=0` fails INTERMITTENTLY (~10-40% of runs) | open | ⬜ (flake, not a clouds regression) | 2026-11-24: fails on origin/main 9529dc3 too, and on every commit sampled back to before V302 (fa58050); pass / fail does not track load (9-40). Only set `shadow-masked` (mask 1) on format `bvh4-single-instanced`, both scenes. Bad rays come in runs of consecutive indices, totals mostly multiples of 32 (64, 160, 192, 224; also 1 or 2): whole 32-lane subgroups. The batch tracer says miss; the page's INLINE kernel (`rtClosestCone` + `rtVisible` + `rtSurface` ALL, workgroup 64) returns a zeroed record (t 0, instance 0, triangle 0, flags 3, material 0xffffffff, cone width = CONE.width), so `coneMismatch` always equals `inlineMismatch`. Theories (untested): Metal / Tint miscompile of the big inline kernel, `rtBoundWalkLoops` cap, an unwritten / racy output slot. Next: log inline + batch record, ray and mask on failure; re-dispatch the inline kernel several times in one page and diff (nondeterminism on identical input = GPU / compiler); try mask ALL for that set; bisect the kernel with `rtBoundWalkLoops` off and `rtSurface` reduced. Until then a trace-core failure counts only if it fails 3 of 3 reruns. Issue [#29](https://github.com/pmndrs/pathtracer/issues/29). |

Tiled f16 vs f32 1.33× (Chrome) / 1.31× (Vulkan). ReLU round trip (`roundTrip=skip`) saves only ~4% on NVIDIA (13.35-14.0 vs 13.9-14.0; Apple 12-24%). **Training step (16k, f16):** Vulkan tiled 0.69-0.70 ms (fwd-bwd 0.51-0.54, gradient 0.15-0.19, Adam 0.009), **sgm ×4 0.74-0.86 vs tiled 0.72-0.80 in the same A/B (tiled/sgm 0.85-1.06): no gain**; Chrome tiled f16 0.84-0.88, f32 0.98-1.03; Vulkan tiled f32 0.91. **Answer to Q3:** on tensor cores, through Dawn, subgroup matrices make inference 1.67× faster than our tiled kernel and about 1.2× faster than the best portable kernel, and do nothing for training: at ~3 TFLOP/s the matrix kernel runs at a small fraction of the tensor-core peak, limited by something other than the ReLU round trip (next suspects: the in-kernel encoding, weight loads per tile). And Windows Chrome users cannot use it at all (N0.3). Raw: `bench/results/mlp-timing.csv` (rows `dawn.node/0.6.2 vulkan ...` and `Chrome/157 headless`, device nvidia/ampere); the first chain of the session was cut by closing Chrome mid-run and is not quoted |
| N2r | Is the 5-6× inference gap to VkNRC closable through WGSL subgroup matrices? Roofline + f16 accumulators on the RTX 3060 Laptop | agent (Windows) | ✅ measured; MLP kernel rewrite open (N2r-b) | **2026-11-16**, Dawn/Vulkan (dawn.node 0.6.2, toggles `allow_unsafe_apis,vulkan_enable_f16_on_nvidia`), 3 ABBA rounds per A/B, card 48-57 °C at each start, no row `loaded` (CPU 0.5-0.7, background apps only). New page `sgm-roofline` (f16 operands, `acc=f32|f16`): `mode=peak` = a × b register-resident tiles multiplied in a constant-bounded loop; `mode=chain` = the MLP's data flow (6 layers of 64×64, activations in workgroup memory) with no encoding, ReLU or biases, checked exactly (cyclic-shift weights). New option `acc=f16` (`pickSubgroupMatrixConfig(..., accumulate)`; page arg on `mlp-timing` / `mlp-check`) picks the f16→f16 16×16×16 config; `mlp-check infer=sgm forward=sgm subgroups=4 acc=f16` passes (inference rel. error 1.6e-3 vs 1.4e-3 with f32 sums, gradient 1.1e-2 vs 4.4e-3). VkNRC accumulates in f16 too (all its cooperative matrices are 16-bit). **Results:** (1) **Dawn reaches the tensor cores:** peak 21.3 TFLOP/s with f32 sums, **42.4-42.6 with f16 sums (2.0×, the GeForce Ampere rate)**. (2) The MLP-shaped chain as our MLP lays it out (4 subgroups, stride 64, f32 sums, one left load per MMA) runs 3.1-3.3 TFLOP/s, the same as the real MLP (~3): **the limit is the data flow, not encoding, ReLU, Dawn or Tint.** (3) Best chain found: **3.60-3.63 ms per M records = 13.5-13.7 TFLOP/s** with f16 sums, ONE subgroup per workgroup (64 records = 4 row blocks), row stride 64 + 8 halves, the layer's 16 right tiles held in registers (`tiling=block`). Paired factors against it: f32 sums 15.7-15.8 ms (**4.4×**: the f32 result buffer + convert pass and 37 KB of workgroup memory per one-subgroup workgroup); 4 subgroups per workgroup 8.5-9.8 (**2.4-2.7×**); stride 64 without pad 4.76 (**1.32×**, bank conflicts); per-output-block tiling (`row`) 3.98 (1.1×); weights copied to workgroup memory are slower (16.5 vs 6.7 at 128 records). (4) In the current MLP kernel f16 sums alone do nothing (13.8-13.9 vs 14.0-19.0 ms/M, `mlp-timing --ab acc=f32,f16 infer=sgm subgroups=4`): its layout is the bottleneck. **Answer:** VkNRC does 2.35-2.47 ms/M including encoding and resolve; the bare chain at 3.6 is within 1.5× of that, so most of the 5.6-6.0× gap is closable in WGSL on NVIDIA/Vulkan by changing the kernel layout. Still unknown: what the ReLU round trip (f16 → f16, in place) and the encoding add back on top of 3.6. Raw: `bench/results/sgm-roofline.csv` (the A/B rows; earlier rows of the same day are a parameter sweep with `--load-wait 0`), `mlp-timing.csv`, `mlp-check.csv` |
| N2r-b | Rewrite the sgm inference kernel in the roofline's layout | agent (Windows) | ✅ NVIDIA; Apple and the default open (N2r-c) | **2026-11-16**, RTX 3060 Laptop, Dawn/Vulkan, card 48-58 °C at each start, no row `loaded`. New `SgmOptions.layout: 'blocked'` (+ `pad`, default 8; page args `layout=blocked pad=8` on `mlp-timing`, `mlp-check`, `nrc-check`, which also takes `sgmRecords`): every right tile of a layer held in registers, each row block loads its left tiles once and feeds one accumulator per column block, row stride 64 + pad; with f16 sums the result goes straight into a second activation buffer (ping-pong) and ReLU runs in place (no convert pass); f32 sums keep activations + results and the convert pass. Native only. Default stays `row` until Apple is measured. **Correct:** `mlp-check` inference rel. error identical to the row layout (1.56e-3 f16 sums, 1.43e-3 f32); `nrc-check` (closed room, true 2.0) passes with `acc=f16 layout=blocked subgroups=1 sgmRecords=32`: mean 2.00007, mean rel. error 3.4e-5, max 1.4e-3 (old kernel 4.1e-5 / 1.6e-3). **Timing (`mlp-timing`, ms per 1M queries, f16 operands):** old default (row, f32 sums, 4 subgroups, 64 records) **13.89-14.00** vs **new (blocked, f16 sums, 1 subgroup, 32 records, pad 8) 5.67-5.68: 2.45-2.47×**, six A B B A pairs. Paired factors against the new kernel (3 ABBA rounds each): row layout 9.2-11.5 (1.6-2.0×), f32 sums 7.58 (1.34×), 64 records 7.41 (1.31×), no padding 8.70 (1.53×). **Gap to native:** VkNRC 2.35-2.47 ms/M with its encoding: now 2.3-2.4× (was 5.6-6.0×); Chrome D3D12's best (per-record, 16.9-18.9) is 3.0-3.3× slower, cross-backend. The roofline chain at 64 records reads 3.6 but the real kernel 7.4 there and 5.7 at 32: the extra is the in-kernel encoding (a 64-float private array per record, suspected of raising register use) and the five ReLU passes. Raw: `bench/results/mlp-timing.csv` (`blocked pad` in the arm description) |
| N2r-c | Blocked sgm kernel, second pass: where the time goes, then the levers | agent (Windows) | ✅ NVIDIA; Apple, default and training open (N2r-d) | **2026-11-16**, RTX 3060 Laptop, Dawn/Vulkan, card 48-57 °C at each start, no row `loaded`. Probes (TIMING ONLY, `mlp-timing` args): `inputProbe=skip` (no encoding: 5.65 vs 5.68 ms/M, the encoding is free), `inputProbe=none` (no input stores), `roundTrip=skip` (no ReLU). At 64 records the matrix work alone was 2.64 ms/M but ReLU cost 4.5: its `for (var i = lid; ...)` loops get Tint's 64-bit loop counter (runtime start). **Changes:** every lane loop of the blocked kernel (ReLU, input, output) is constant-trip (`laneLoop`: `for (var j = 0u; j < N; j++) { let i = lid + j * wg; ... }`), and ReLU handles `reluRun` adjacent halves per item (default 4; 1, 2, 8 allowed). With that 64 records beats 32. **Paired (ms per 1M queries, f16):** old default (row, f32 sums, 4 subgroups) 13.85-18.74 (median 13.9) vs **new (blocked, f16 sums, 1 subgroup, 64 records, reluRun 4) 3.85 in all six A B B A pairs: 3.6×**; reluRun 1 → 4: 4.63 → 3.85; 32 → 64 records: 5.35 → 3.85; ReLU skipped: 2.67 (ReLU = 1.18 ms, 31%). **Gap to VkNRC inference (2.35-2.47 with its encoding): 1.56-1.64×** (5.6-6.0× before N2r). The matrix work alone (2.67) is within 1.1× of VkNRC's whole pass; the remaining gap is the ReLU round trip WGSL forces (no element access, gpuweb #11077). **Correct:** `mlp-check` inference error unchanged (1.56e-3); `nrc-check` passes at 64 records (mean 2.00027, rel. error 1.3e-4, max 1.6e-3) and 32 (7.3e-5 / 1.4e-3). Raw: `bench/results/mlp-timing.csv` |

Render kernel and trainer kernel are the plain kernel's options + one hook (`NRC_HOOK_WGSL`): render paths terminate into the cache at vertex `queryDepth` and append a query; inference + resolve add throughput × prediction into the accumulator (new `PtMegakernel.accumulator` getter). Training targets by deterministic replays on a small trainer film (M + 1 passes per frame, cache as the tail; N-I9). **Gate (`nrc-check`, closed emissive room, true answer 2.0):** after 300 frames, M5 Pro: portable f32 mean error 0.10% (query at 2nd hit) / 0.06% (1st hit), f16 0.55% / 0.52%, subgroup matrix f16 ×4 0.22%, f32 ×1 0.22%; worst pixel ≤ 1.7%; untrained cache reads 1.58 (the check discriminates). Open: the wavefront scheduler (its shade kernel leaves 2 storage buffers: the hook fits); a path-end hook in main would cut the trainer to one pass (V84) |

Sponza and Cornell. Verified in the app's browser pane (portable f16): renders, image means plain 0.242 vs NRC 0.245 at 235 spp, NRC visibly less noisy. In-pane Benchmark (best effort, shared machine): 4 training steps 14.6 ms, cache-side render 13.0, inference + resolve 59.4 (portable f16, 503k queries), plain 36.0 ms per spp at 960×540. **Flagged Chrome (2026-11-15, subgroup matrix f16 ×4, wall clock per stage, best effort):** 960×540: training paths 4.3, 4 steps 14.8, cache-side render 13.5, inference + resolve 6.7 = 39 ms; plain 1 spp 37 ms. **Real-time setup** (button: 640×360, cache view only, 2 steps): **40 fps**, 19.7 ms = training paths 4.2 + steps 6.9 + render 5.7 + inference 2.9. NrcCache `training: 'inline'` (default; 'replay' kept): training paths ride in the render pass, the hook records their vertices and sums their light per vertex, the tail is an ordinary query. `trainSelect: 'tile'` (default) trains whole 8×8 tiles (every 16th, rotating) so no workgroup waits on a long training path; 'pixel' = main's per-pixel updateBlock rule. Room gate (300 frames): f32 0.05%, f16 0.10%, subgroup matrix f16 0.003% mean error. **Sponza 640×360, subgroup matrix f16, 2 steps, cache side per frame:** tiles 9.0 ms (render 5.6, steps 1.3, inference 2.1), pixels 11.0-11.3, replays 11.4-11.6 (3.0 of replays). Sponza 960×540 after 300 frames, 256 spp each: image mean plain 0.2416 vs cache 0.2421 (+0.2%). **Wavefront (2026-11-15):** escape, emission and terminate report from the shade kernel; NEE from the shadow kernel after its visibility test, which binds only the hook's `radianceBindings` as its group 3 (`setHookRadianceBindGroup`; room for 3 storage buffers on Apple, 1 with an exact alpha-mask shadow policy) and reads (vertex, flags, sample, pixel) from a 4th shadow-ray section that exists only with the callback. All 133 snapshot kernels (95 wavefront) byte-identical to before; `pt-outputs-check radiance` on the wavefront: per vertex 1.2e-7, NEE + RR sum 3.9e-7. Not checked: refill mode (`refill`, `layers` > 1). The NRC cache itself still runs on the megakernel |

| N6.1 | VkNRC native on the RTX 3060L as the native timing baseline (W11): build under `.cache/native/`, local patches for per-pass timestamps, fixed camera, PFM dump | agent (Windows) | ✅ native numbers; web side of the per-pass ratio waits for WN5 | **Done 2026-11-16** (D28/D29: built and run only under `.cache/native/vknrc`, nothing of it committed). Clone of `a667859`, x64 Release with VS 2022 17.10 + Vulkan SDK 1.3.283 as its `windows-msvc.yml` (unset `CMAKE_TOOLCHAIN_FILE` first, Traps). **Local patches, in words:** (1) the render-graph runner writes a GPU timestamp after every pass and the app reads them back once a frame's fence has signalled, one CSV row per pass and frame, plus the frame's inference-query and training-record counts read from their host-mapped buffers; (2) command-line camera pose, method (both halves), accumulation start frame and frame count, then exit; (3) the accumulate image gets transfer-source usage and is written as PFM after the last frame. **Run:** RTX 3060 Laptop, driver 592.00, 1280×720 window, `sponza-white-vknrc` OBJ from WN6 (145,050 vertices, 262,266 triangles), camera from its `vknrc.json`, NRC on both halves, EMA off (VkNRC default), 300 frames per run, 6 runs per method alternated (nrc, none, none, nrc), medians over frames 50-299. AC power, Chrome/Cursor closed; the card ran hot (48-86 °C at start, 1.5-2.05 GHz) but the runs agree within 4%. **Per frame (ms):** NRC total 5.07-5.28 = V-buffer 0.10-0.11 + path tracer 1.59-1.66 + inference 2.15-2.26 (914.5k queries: **2.35-2.47 ms per million**, including the NRC encoding from scene data and the resolve) + training 4 × 16,384 records: fused forward-backward-gradient **0.254-0.263 per batch**, optimizer 0.006 per batch (about 1.07 for the four) + screen 0.16. Plain path tracing (`none`, 8 hits, hardware ray queries): path tracer 8.18-8.43, frame 8.55-8.80. **Images:** VkNRC NRC (frames 50-299 accumulated) mean within -0.2% (R, B) / -2.2% (G) of its own 4096-frame path-traced image; our megakernel on `sponza-white-vknrc` (1024 spp) has mean 0.30411 vs that image's 0.30390 (+0.07%): the WN6 lighting match holds. PFMs: `.cache/wn7/vknrc-pt-4096.pfm` (native reference for the VkNRC lighting), `run1-nrc.pfm`. **Web / native, first cut** (same laptop, not paired in one run; web numbers from the WN3 microbench, image-task encoding): inference per million queries Dawn/Vulkan sgm 13.9-14.0 = **5.6-6.0×** native, Chrome D3D12 per-record 16.9-18.9 = 6.8-8.0×, tiled 23.8-24.1 = 9.6-10.3×; training per 16k batch Dawn/Vulkan tiled 0.69-0.70 = **2.6×**, sgm 0.74-0.86 = 2.8-3.3×, Chrome tiled f16 0.84-0.88 = 3.1-3.4×. The in-frame web numbers (cache inference + resolve, trainer, render pass) come from WN5's Benchmark on `sponza-white-vknrc` at 1280×720. **Path tracing is the big gap, and it is ours:** our megakernel took 397-425 ms/spp on `sponza-white-vknrc` at 720p (runner says thermal-throttled, but clocks stayed at 1.7 GHz), and 195 ms/spp on `sponza-white`, where the M5 Pro reads 27-50: 4-7× slower than the Mac on this GPU (new row P-W1) |
| P-W1 | Megakernel 3-4× slower on the RTX 3060 (Chrome D3D12) than on Vulkan or the M5 Pro | agent (Windows) | ✅ fixed (Mac to confirm no Metal regression) | **Found and fixed 2026-11-16.** Symptom: `sponza-white` 191-202 ms/spp in Chrome (M5 Pro 56-63); `sponza-white-vknrc` ~400 at 720p. **Not the hardware or traversal:** `trace-timing` on the 3060 reads 0.75-0.86× the Mac (primary 229 vs 285 Mrays/s, bounce 102 vs 137), the wavefront scheduler does Sponza at 52 ms/spp, and the same megakernel WGSL on Dawn's Vulkan backend (runner `--dawn vulkan`) runs 60-65 ms/spp. dawn.node on D3D12 reproduces Chrome (200-215). **Cause:** Tint's `PreventInfiniteLoops` (part of robustness) injects a 64-bit iteration counter (`vec2u`, decremented with a borrow) into every loop it cannot prove finite; inside the megakernel both BVH walks are inlined into the bounce loop, and DXC's Shader Model 6.6+ code for the descent and pop `loop {}` of bvh's walk with those counters is 3.7× slower on NVIDIA. Toggle bisection on dawn.node D3D12 at depth 1 (megakernel / wavefront ms per spp): default 68.1 / 17.7, `disable_robustness` 15.7 / 19.9, `d3d12_dont_use_shader_model_66_or_higher` 18.9 / 20.0 (identical HLSL, different DXC target), FXC 34.4 / 36.5, IEEE strictness and workgroup init no effect; the stack-index clamps (`min(i, 63)`) are not it (removing them changed nothing), nor the bounce loop's own counter; NEE off still 3.9×. Only loops of the form `for (var i = <int>; i <op> <constant>; i++)` with the bound test first escape the counter (Tint `loop_analysis`; the bound must be a literal constant). **Fix:** `rtBoundWalkLoops` (`packages/raytracer/src/trace/modules.ts`) rewrites the walk's four `loop {}` as `for (var rtWalkStepN = 0u; rtWalkStepN < 0xffffffffu; rtWalkStepN++)` and adds the final return each walk then needs (V143); `walk-loops.test.ts` checks it applies to all 48 walk variants. Result (Chrome 157, D3D12, sponza-white, 32 spp × 3 in-page rounds): **megakernel 58.0-60.7 ms/spp (was 191-202, 3.3×)**, wavefront 49.5-51.8, same image (max rel 4.5e-7); Dawn Vulkan unchanged (59.4-63.5). `trace-core` passes (0 mismatches). Kernels grow ~130 B (size caps in `integrator-wgsl.test.ts` raised 150 B). **Open:** the Mac must check Metal does not regress (`wf-sched-ab scene=sponza-white renderers=pt-megakernel,pt-wavefront`, before/after); ask bvh to write its walk loops in this shape (filed as https://github.com/pmndrs/bvh/issues/3; then `rtBoundWalkLoopsApplies` turns false, the test fails, and the transform can go). The remaining counters (triangle loops bounded by a runtime `count`, one search loop) cost nothing measurable here |
| N6.2 | OBJ exporter for sponza-white (VkNRC input), and a "VkNRC lighting" setting on the `nrc` page (white sky 10, no sun) for like-for-like images | agent:nrc-windows | ✅ (VkNRC run: WN7) | **Done 2026-11-15 (cloud session, WN6; no GPU there).** `bench/exporters/obj.ts` + `node bench/exporters/export-obj.ts [scene]` (default `sponza-white-vknrc`) → `bench/assets/generated/vknrc/<scene>/scene.obj`, `scene.mtl`, `vknrc.json`. Read from VkNRC's loader (described, not copied): it reads no normals (flat geometric, two-sided), refuses an OBJ without a material, takes `Kd`/`Ks`/`Ke`/`Pr` (Cook-Torrance; `Ks 0` = Lambert) and rescales the model to [-1, 1]; so we write positions (bit-identical ones welded: 145,050 vertices, 262,266 triangles, 10 MB) and one `usemtl` per mesh; `vknrc.json` holds VkNRC's centre/scale and the scene camera as its normalised position, yaw, pitch and fov for the fixed-camera patch (WN7). tinyobjloader parses it with 1 material and no bad ids. Lighting: new scene `sponza-white-vknrc` (sponza-white geometry/camera, constant sky 10, no sun, `maxDepth` 7 = VkNRC's 8-hit loop, no RR; nrc-diverges N-I7/N-I11); `nrc-live` scene menu, `nrc-study --args lighting=vknrc` (its references from `bench/references/sponza-white-vknrc/` when rendered, else our own PT at `refSpp`). Export its glb: `node bench/adapters/export-scenes.ts sponza-white-vknrc`. Check: Mitsuba 3.9.1 at 320×180, 256 spp, mean 0.3044 (see the nrc-plan log for ours on SwiftShader). Native references not rendered (`node bench/references/render.ts --scenes sponza-white-vknrc`) |

| N3b | Subgroup-matrix training | agent:nrc | ✅ | **Audit 2026-11-26:** the sgm weight gradient landed in N2r-d (6eaf7c2, 81cf415); the Apple A/B is N2r-e. **Forward-backward done 2026-11-15** (`wgsl-sgm-train.ts`, engine `forward: 'sgm'` + `trainSgm`; emulated twin `sgm-emu` passes on SwiftShader and the GPU). Native compiled first time (Chrome 157, M5 Pro, 4 subgroups via `subgroup_id`). Backward reads W as a row-major right operand (no transposed copy); ReLU and its mask go through workgroup memory. Oracle: f32 gradient 1.6e-7 (= tiled), f16 8.6e-4 (tiled 8.1e-4); f16 replay p99 0.035 vs tiled 0.015 (Apple's only f16 config accumulates in f16). **Timing (M5 Pro, best effort, noisy medians: quote min/p25):** forward-backward f32 tiled 0.62-0.67 → sgm ×4 0.40-0.42 ms (**1.55× faster**); f16 tiled 0.31 → sgm ×4 0.34-0.40 ms (**10-25% slower**); 1 subgroup is 2-3× slower than 4. So on Apple a well-tiled plain kernel is about as fast as the matrix path; defaults stay `tiled` (the NRC cache trains in f16). Follow-ups: sgm weight-gradient pass; Adam writing the padded weights; the real test is NVIDIA tensor cores (M6) |

| N11.14 | Chain debug bench (`bench/pages/chain-debug.html`, `node bench/live.ts chain-debug`): the Classroom chain with a view of every pass (frame planes, guides, SVGF history length / accumulation / anti-lag λ / outputs, OIDN inputs, reactive mask, final), nearest zoom + per-pixel readout of every pass, scripted camera motion, pinned views, a reprojected flicker map and "Flicker over 60 frames" per stage; every SVGF option live (`PipelineArm.setDenoiserOptions`); camera jitter switch (`integrator.cameraJitter: 'engine'`, `PtMegakernel.setCameraJitter`, megakernel only, default kernels unchanged) | agent | 🟨 built 2026-11-26 | First numbers (browser pane, 640 × 360, NRC arm, default Classroom camera, one 60-frame run each, mean relative reprojected |ΔL| / L per frame): still camera, per-pixel jitter: denoised 0.215, final 0.009; jitter off (pixel centre): denoised 0.053, final 0.0045; per-frame Halton 8 (unknown to SVGF): same as per-pixel. So when still, the per-pixel jitter is most of the edge flicker (a thin edge flips between surfaces in the 1-spp guides). Strafing 0.15 m: final 0.036 whatever the jitter or SVGF option (fallback strict, normal test center, anti-lag off: no change); bilinear / spatial upscaling 0.11-0.15 (the temporal upscaler helps), reactive mask 0.088 (worse), upscaler history 8: 0.044; the denoised image boils at 0.13 while moving vs 0.05 still, so the motion boil is SVGF history loss, not the upscaler; motion vectors check out (reprojected normal / viewZ change halves). Open: (1) why SVGF loses history under motion: read the history-length view while strafing, at the user's two spots (coat rack / chair corner; back-right chair, the floor under the chairs and the desk-tray edges, 2026-11-26) - pin them in the page; (2) the jitter answer for real: centre aliases, so a per-frame jitter that SVGF and the upscaler are told about (N11.2); (3) quote these again from a visible window (not the throttled pane). **Still camera, second pass (2026-11-26, relative reprojected flicker, 640 × 360, NRC arm):** the package's default anti-lag (A-SVGF 'auto') holds SVGF's history at p50 ~6 frames (22-36% of hit pixels at <= 4) on a STILL view, although λ is tiny (p50 0.02): A-SVGF resets n to 1/α on any boost; plain PT the same, training off the same. Anti-lag off: p50 64 (the cap). `antiLagHistoryScale: true` (the package's own fix): p50 38-45. The other half is remodulation: the albedo factor is one jittered sample, so on textured wood it changes 11% per frame at a still camera, and the remodulated SVGF diffuse flickers 0.186 against 0.10 for its demodulated history. Denoised / final flicker, still: today 0.216 / 0.0092; jitter off 0.054 / 0.0042; jitter off + anti-lag off + α 0.05: 0.012 / 0.0018; jitter off + anti-lag with history scale: 0.035-0.044. Strafing with jitter off and full history (p50 60): denoised still 0.097, the albedo / normal guides themselves 6-8% per frame: texture aliasing of centre sampling, which a per-frame jitter known to every stage is for (N11.15). Input to SVGF: per frame, diffuse-class radiance / mean albedo factor and specular-class radiance / mean specular factor (1 spp, fresh every frame unless 'accumulate while still'), guides (normal, viewZ, depth, motion, motionZ, roughness, albedo factor) from the frame's last sample; demodulating by albedo is SVGF's own design, the problem is that the albedo is a jittered single sample. |

Package default (auto): p50 5.7, 77% at <= 8 frames. D (auto + history scale): p50 38, p10 6, 13% at <= 8, 92% below the cap. Anti-lag off: 91% at the cap, the other 9% thin lines on every silhouette (chair legs, window frames: the per-frame jitter moves an edge pixel's sample to the other surface and the normal test rejects it; plane distance 0.03 changes nothing). `normalTest: 'relative'` keeps them: 1.7% below the cap (soft 8.9%, center 8.9%). | N11.17 | Guide inputs under jitter, and SVGF history at silhouettes / thin geometry (maintainer, 2026-11-27: settle the guide inputs first) | agent | 🟨 measured 2026-11-27; texture cone LOD is the session default since 2026-11-27 | chain-debug probes `__chainDebug.guideChange` / `edgeHistory` / `edgeQuality`, session option `textureLod` ('cone', scale) + page control "Texture LOD", page controls for the Denoiser branch option `historyFootprint`. Classroom, 640 x 360 -> 1280 x 720, NRC arm, still camera, single runs, browser pane, load 2.4-4.3. **Guides** (8 frame pairs; edge = within 1 px of a depth step > 3 % or a normal cosine < 0.95, 46-52 % of this scene): jitter centre: normal, viewZ, roughness, specular factor and albedo factor rgb are bit-identical frame to frame on both arms (no Monte Carlo noise in a guide); the only stochastic thing in a plane is the class flag (albedo alpha, V122: the lobe sampled at the primary vertex), 1.6-2.4 % of pixels flip per frame, on metal. With jitter, interior: normal 5e-5 (0.01 % of pixel-frames turn > 18 deg), viewZ 0.14 %, roughness and specular factor exactly 0, albedo factor 8.7 % at mip 0 (31 % of pixel-frames over 5 %) -> 3.0 % with `textureLod: 'cone'` 1 px (16 %) -> 1.7 % at 2 px (7.8 %); what is left is print and joints inside flat surfaces (posters, blackboard, ceiling grid: sharp albedo steps), not a plane bug. The scene has no normal / roughness maps (those planes do not move with the LOD). Edge pixels (upscaler jitter): normal 0.114 (23 % over 18 deg), viewZ 1.5 % (5.8 % over 2 %), albedo 14.9 % -> 10.4 % (cone): the sample lands on another surface, inherent. Cone LOD downstream (setting D, still): denoised flicker on stable-guide pixels 0.046 -> 0.028, final 0.00088 -> 0.00077; whole image final 0.00284 -> 0.00264 (cone 2 px 0.00268); strafe 0.0254 -> 0.0249. **Recommended: `textureLod: 'cone'` (scale 1) for the real-time session** (not flipped here). **History**: the resets do not follow from texture aliasing (8.7 % below the cap, mip 0 and cone alike). Root cause confirmed per pixel: every below-cap pixel is one whose guides changed surface in the window (100 % / 0 % stable), none with jitter off; 24.6 % of hit pixels flip, SVGF's 3 x 3 fallback rescues two thirds (`historyFallback: 'off'`: 24.7 % below the cap); it is the normal test (`normalThreshold` 0: 0.5 %; `planeDistance` 0.1: no change; 'relative' 1.7 %). The teal patch by the window is a corrugated panel finer than a pixel (normals swing (-1, 0, 0) .. (0, 0, 1) inside one pixel at 2.91-2.94 m): same cause, no neighbour ever matches within 18 deg. **Built in the Denoiser** (worktree `~/Developer/Denoiser/.claude/worktrees/chain-edge-history`, branch `fix/jitter-edge-history`, its results/JITTER-EDGE-HISTORY.md): `historyFootprint: 'neighbour' / 'plane' / 'both'` (default off): where a pixel would restart, keep footprint taps whose surface a neighbour still shows this frame, or that lie within the plane distance whatever the normal; capped at 1 / motion (px per frame). Below the cap (anti-lag off) 8.8 % -> 4.0 / 2.6 / 0.5 %, <= 8 frames 6.2 % -> 2.3 / 1.1 / 0.2 %; on D <= 8 frames 11.7-14 % -> 8.6 % (plane) / 7.7 % (both), the rest is anti-lag's (half of it on stable pixels). **Not adopted**: on the pixels that lose history (8.8 %) D + plane lowers the final flicker 0.00518 -> 0.00462 (-11 %) and raises the error of the time-averaged denoised image against a 2048-spp pixel-area reference 0.115 -> 0.129 (+12 %, bias -0.5 % -> +1.5 %): the kept history was shaded on the other surface. D + both: flicker 0.00456, error 0.146, and more ghosting (pan relMSE 0.00409 -> 0.00485; plane 0.00435; strafe 0.00390 / 0.00392 / 0.00431). Whole image D / D + plane / D + both: still final 0.00284 / 0.00270 / 0.00259, strafe final 0.0254 / 0.0255 / 0.0248, lamp r1 / r8 / r32 0.133 / 0.075 / 0.038 vs 0.137 / 0.075 / 0.040 vs 0.137 / 0.077 / 0.041 (4 frames to 10 % each). **Why little can be won there:** with 16 spp per frame (noise / 4) the final flicker on those pixels only falls 0.0052 -> 0.0044 (-14 %): ~85 % of it is the jitter moving the sample between surfaces (the denoised colour changes 0.48 relative per frame there, by design), which the upscaler integrates; upscaler `lockThinFeatures` off: +7 %, `detectShadingChanges` off: -3 %. A footprint history in modulated radiance (to remove the albedo x irradiance covariance bias) was built and diverges through the spatial filter (error x 40): dropped. Local link: `bench/node_modules/denoiser` -> the Denoiser worktree (restore: `ln -sfn ../../../Denoiser/packages/denoiser bench/node_modules/denoiser`; on main's build the page's footprint control is ignored). Open: (1) flip the session to cone LOD and re-take D; (2) the stochastic class flag on metals (a deterministic split, e.g. both lobes' shares, would remove a noise source of the two signals); (3) the upscaler's edge handling at 2x from 360p is where the remaining still-edge flicker is; (4) anti-lag's lambda on stable pixels is the larger still-camera history limit on D. **Default flip (2026-11-27):** `NrcPipelineSession` now builds with `textureLod: 'cone'` and a cone start of one DISPLAY pixel (`textureConeScale` default render / display width = 0.5 at 2x; pass `textureLod: 0` for the old mip-0 chain, which every chain study before this date used). Classroom, D settings, still, upscaler jitter, NRC arm (denoised / final flicker, settled-final detail = mean abs Laplacian / mean luminance, whole / bottom third): mip 0 0.200 / 0.00279, 0.293 / 0.302; cone 1 render px 0.164 / 0.00261, 0.281 / **0.250** (wood visibly soft on the chair backs); cone 0.5 (= one display px) 0.173 / 0.00264, 0.288 / 0.278 (grain kept by eye); cone 2: 0.159 / 0.00272, 0.275 / 0.229. SVGF history unchanged by any of them (p50 ~37, 13% at <= 8). A cone of one render pixel is too wide behind a 2x upscaler (the usual upscaler mip bias is log2(render / display)). |
