| N12.4 | Run phase 2–5 Mac experiments | — | ✅ | Adaptive termination and confidence, frozen inference, active hash maintenance, and evaluated material inputs. [Bench results and remaining platform comparisons](bench/results/NRC-ADAPTIVE.md). |
| N12.5 | Measure reflectance factorization and compact MLP on the Mac | — | ✅ | Evaluated inline targets, saturated textures and rough metals. Both portable fp16 kernels, glossy Sponza, a live sun edit, and shape-specific reset and reload. [Measurements and controls](bench/results/NRC-FACTOR-COMPACT.md). |
| N12.6 | Test reflected direction with compact raw-RGB NRC | — | ✅ | Two Mac camera sweeps, independent direction oracle, and live toggle. [Phase 8 measurements](bench/results/NRC-REFLECTED-VIEW.md). |
| N12.7 | Isolate wall stripes, contact shadows and missing reflections, then sweep the training budget | — | ✅ | Mac tile, step and suffix A/Bs, held-out paths and edit recovery. Live demo can progressively switch to full paths. [Phase 9 measurements](bench/results/NRC-SHADOWS-REFLECTIONS.md). |
| N12.8 | Phase 10 directional radiance hash | — | ✅ Mac | Equal-memory RGB, direction bins and SH against compact MLP. Fixed duplicate concurrent inserts. [Reflection, locality and wall noise](bench/results/NRC-DIRECTIONAL-HASH.md). |
| N12.9 | Phase 11 learned multiresolution position features | — | ✅ Mac | Equal-time and frozen comparisons against the same compact decoder and direct radiance hash. [Spatial features](bench/results/NRC-SPATIAL-FEATURES.md). |
| N12.10 | Phase 12 reflections hit-shading consumer | — | ✅ Mac bench | Verified direct/indirect accounting, hit distance, confidence, emission and edit recovery through the provider. [Reflections consumer](bench/results/NRC-REFLECTION-CONSUMER.md). |
| N12.11 | Phase 13 prefiltered incident radiance | — | ✅ Mac fixture | Grazing highlight bias limits the split-sum target. [Results](bench/results/NRC-PREFILTERED-SPECULAR.md). |
| N12.12 | Phases 14–15 WebNN compact inference replay | Windows WebGPU / WebNN / results agents | ✅ Mac / ✅ Windows | Actual changing weights, renderer records, CPU oracle and GPU-visible predictions. Windows RTX 3060, Chrome 157, quiet run (timing VALID): WebNN replay is 15–16 ms p50 at 8,192 rows and 28–29 ms at 4,096, against 3.0–3.6 ms for WGSL per-record (4.4–9.7×), and the whole NRC frame is 3.0–3.8 ms. Parity is 0.07–0.10% rRMS. **Keep portable WGSL.** Chrome's ORT/DirectML backend needs a bias on `gemm` (`gemmC=zero`, default on Windows). The NPU request falls back silently on a machine without an NPU. The WN8 tiled vs per-record wall-clock times tie: settle the kernel with GPU timestamps if needed. [WN8–WN11 and run commands](docs/design/nrc-windows-handoff.md#phase-15-agent-queue). [Results](bench/results/NRC-WEBNN-COMPACT.md#windows-rtx-3060-phase-15-wn8wn11). |
| N12.13 | Hash cache insert: one cell, one slot under concurrent inserts (pmndrs/pathtracer#1, reported from gi `p6-sharc`) | — | ✅ model + SwiftShader / 🟨 real GPU | The race itself was fixed in N12.8 (0e4435b). Left open there: an unbounded `loop {}` around the weak exchange (no forward-progress bound, and a Tint loop counter on D3D12). `hashGridInsertWgsl` now retries an empty slot at most 4 times, then REFUSES the insert (moving on could give the cell a second slot). GPU-free interleaving model in `hash-grid.test.ts`: the dd771c8 insert duplicates cells, the current one never does, never refuses with room at p = 0, and the model fails if exhaustion moves on. `--page hash-insert-check` (the issue's suggested check) runs the real text plus the dd771c8 control: SwiftShader passes, but the control also reads 0 duplicates there. **Open:** one run on the Mac / RTX 3060, where the control should show duplicates. |
| N12.14 | WebNN ↔ WebGPU hand-off study: can a better sync close the 44 ms vs 0.4 ms gap? (MLTensor explainer: context from the GPUDevice, a tensor pair per chunk with batched exports, dropping the drain wait, and the script route as the naive baseline) | — | 🟨 Mac measured 2026-11-24, Windows run open | **Mac (M5 Pro, Chrome 158, load 2.9-4.2, best effort): the `exportToGPU` hand-off was the cost.** Default 44-50 ms; `sync=none` 37-40 ms but wrong answers; `tensors=chunk` 39-40 ms; `ctx=device` no change; `route=cpu` (through JavaScript) **3.0 ms**, 2.7-2.8 ms with `tensors=chunk`, parity intact, gpu = npu. One dispatch per frame (review follow-up, load 4.4-5.6): through JavaScript 2.8-3.0 ms, same as six; `exportToGPU` tensors are capped at 8,192 rows (32,768 rejected, 16,384 loses the device) and 3 dispatches = 6 (37-39 ms). Folded tensor (`fold=8`, [4096, 512] reshaped in the graph) = ONE exported dispatch: **6.1-6.9 ms** vs 35-38 (parity intact; `sync=none` then also correct, no gain); script route folded 2.4-2.5 ms. Not tested: training as WebNN graph ops. Still 2x the whole WGSL cache frame (1.1-1.4 ms) and ~6x WGSL inference (0.4-0.5 ms); the expectation "`export` faster" was wrong. Open: the same arms on Windows (RTX 3060); a quiet-Mac rerun if the absolute numbers are to be final. `webnn-replay.ts` args `ctx` / `tensors` / `sync` / `route` (+ `breakdown=1` per-phase times, `parity=report`), defaults = the Nov 19 sequence; option parsing unit-tested, the `cpu` route's WebNN calls probed on Linux. Cannot run in a cloud container (f16 network, no exportable tensors on SwiftShader). Commands, expectations written before any run, and the results table: [NRC-WEBNN-SYNC.md](bench/results/NRC-WEBNN-SYNC.md). Issue [#33](https://github.com/pmndrs/pathtracer/issues/33). |

So it is DXC codegen of the cwbvh8 any-hit walk, not P-W1's loop counter: for the bvh side. HLSL / DXIL dump (reproduce: `node bench/runner/run.ts --page trace-timing --gpu nvidia --dawn d3d12 --dawn-toggles allow_unsafe_apis,dump_shaders --verbose --no-csv --args scene=sponza-white nodeFormat=cwbvh8 timing=batch samples=2`; the closest-hit and any-hit kernels are the two "Dumped generated HLSL" blocks): both kernels have the same skeleton (3 loops, the 64-entry stack as one `alloca [64 x i32]`, no Tint loop counters); the any-hit side differs in its leaf tests being `bool` functions that `return true` from inside the nested loop. Hypothesis only (register pressure / control flow DXC handles badly), not measured. Contradicts the M5 suggestion (cwbvh8) for Windows; default stays binary (D33) and nothing was changed here. (a) **Texture mips (done, D34: the GPU path is the library default; `@pmndrs/downsampler` is a dependency of `@pmndrs/raytracer`)**: our CPU chain (`downsampleBytes`) is the only mip producer. `TextureOptions.mips: 'gpu'` + `uploadSceneCopy({ mipGenerator: new Downsampler(device) })` (structural type `RtMipGenerator`) fills levels 1+ on the GPU: `--page downsampler-check` compares every level of every bucket with the CPU chain (level 0 identical, levels 1+ within 2 codes, 4-12% of sRGB bytes and 15-75% of linear bytes differ by 1-2; the library is closer to the exact box mean, 0.28 against 0.45 codes) and times both (24 textures, 1024^2: 250 -> 30 ms, 2048^2: 1007 -> 121 ms, provisional). `pt_mips=gpu` runs a scene end to end (`tex-checker-hf`: per-pixel identical). `mips: 'cpu'` stays as a setting (V295, D34). (b) **Auto-exposure**: `PipelineArm.tonemap('auto')` reduces the `final` stage's mean log luma with `ScalarReduction` (two dispatches, no readback) into the tonemap uniform; `realtime-pipeline autoExposure=1`: GPU 4.48700 against CPU twin 4.48700 (rel 2e-7), `--page downsampler-check` on 1920x1081: 6e-8, texel count exact at an odd size. (c) Hi-Z: nothing in our code builds a depth pyramid (the chain's depth guide goes to the upscaler); the first user will be `gi` / `reflections`, whose own Hi-Z is their business. **Windows (W16) done 2026-11-19**, RTX 3060 Laptop, Chrome 157 / D3D12, driver 592.00 (machine loaded: CPU 75-97% busy, another agent's headless Chrome on the GPU; correctness is not affected, the times are provisional): PASS, level 0 identical, levels 1+ within 2 codes of the CPU chain on every path (`sample`, `load`, and the sRGB render path, forced with `renderPathMinTexels: 1`; `outputPath()` confirms buffer vs render per bucket in `notes.mipsPaths_*`); default generator = explicit Downsampler (0 bytes); exposure 2.8e-7, mean 8e-8. sRGB render vs buffer path: with `averageAccess: 'load'` 68 bytes differ (max 1 code, float ties as the library documents); with the default `'sample'` 63,588 bytes (0.63% of all sRGB bytes, max 2 codes), from NVIDIA's hardware bilinear sampler (the render path samples every level, the buffer path computes in-tile levels exactly): not the library's "same bits", but inside the 2-code bar, so D34 stands on D3D12. Chains of 1920x1080+ texels take the render path by default, i.e. real 2048^2 textures. Load time (wall, loaded CPU): 24 textures 1024^2 1177 -> 213 ms (5.5x), 2048^2 5680 -> 918 ms (6.2x). (2) `rc-interval-check` counted a cross-instance coincident-face tie as an exactness failure (Cornell: box bottom on the floor, same f32 t, both interior; the range changes the walk's child order): `EdgeIndex.coplanarWorld` in `trace-compare.ts` accepts it, the failure note now prints both hits. Open, for the Mac / bvh side (no tolerance was changed here): (a) **trace-core**: ONE ray of 1.33M (Sponza `edges#7294`), identical in all 9 formats, so the triangle test, not the walk: the GPU hits tri 72111 at bary (1.1e-4, 0.318) (interior, t 2.0528), the f64 CPU walk and the RTX 3060 miss it and hit tri 74679 at t 2.0723. 1.1e-4 is far beyond f32 rounding of a sane triangle: Intel's compile of the edge function loses precision (thin triangle or cancellation; cf. the Metal fma trap, `bvhEdgeFunction`). (b) **bsdf-check**: 5 of 18,664 sampled values off by 0.7-1.2% (> 0.005), narrow lobes only (dielectric alpha 0.0625 pdf; conductor alpha 0.01 f and pdf at u.x 0.007): GPU transcendental / normalize precision on a narrow lobe, distribution not checked. (c) **rc-interval-check**: 4 Cornell `f32-indexed` boundary queries where the competing hit is 1 ulp away in t ON A VERTEX (bary ~2e-8, the box corner touching the floor): `sameOrTie` wants bit-equal t; whether a 1-ulp vertex touch is a tie is the interval contract's call. (d) **openpbr-check** passes but reads `aniso_frame_mismatch` 27 / 2000 and `layered_sample_mismatch` 1 (0 on the 3060). `wf-p5-check`: only `vol-vdb-smoke` Issue [#31](https://github.com/pmndrs/pathtracer/issues/31). |

## Docs and examples site (S, 2026-11-20)

Plan reviewed by the maintainer 2026-11-20: pmndrs/docs MDX in `docs/` (internal notes moved to `docs/internal/`), a Vite examples gallery in
`examples/` with categories, an injected nav (pmndrs logo, breadcrumb, status badge, Docs / Source) and a template, Tweakpane for controls, the
glTF loader made public, hand-written API tables, experimental and research features included and labelled on the index and in the nav.

### Phases

| Phase | Scope | State |

## Open questions for the maintainer

- ~~ThreeRT as a competitor: add the native adapter (P0.32) now, later, or never?~~ Settled by D36 (2026-11-26): an optional peer, built when wanted, never required. Notes kept below.
  Old text: **ThreeRT as a competitor: add the native adapter (P0.32) now, later, or never?** Suggestion: later, internal only, after P2.9
  (sponza-white in our renderer), as a "native HW-RT" row in the separate inexact report (D20).
  - Pro: the only HW ray-query path tracer we can run on this Mac; shows what hardware RT buys over our software BVH on the same
    GPU; MIT; a headless CLI with linear f32 output exists.
  - Con: native (child_process, no WebGPU timestamps) so timing is not like-for-like; UE5-matched BSDF and conventions mean a
    bias floor against pbrt/Mitsuba; needs Rust/Bun toolchain and a DSL wrapper per scene; API is moving fast (70 PRs).
  - Settles it: quad-light + Cornell at 1024 spp with all overrides; keep it if mean bias vs Mitsuba is < 1% and relMSE follows
    1/spp.

- ~~Tier 1 gate~~: settled 2026-11-15, D23 (class A only). ~~Default layering~~: D24. ~~Wide BVH default~~: D25 (per scene until the 3060). ~~Kernel size~~: D26 (measure W10 first).
- **NRC: where does the MLP engine live?** Suggestion: private workspace package `packages/mlp` (WGSL prefix `nn*`),
  demo as bench pages until N6; a production cache follows D3 into `@pmndrs/gi`. Details, pros/cons and the deciding
  test in [docs/internal/design/nrc.md](docs/internal/design/nrc.md) §8 (also: gpuweb evidence, Windows Vulkan flag;
  VkNRC licence and native build settled by D28/D29).

- ~~Capsaicin: a third reference renderer, or a cross-check only?~~ Settled by D36 (2026-11-26): an optional peer (equal-time quality, native speed, GI-1.x), never required; pbrt + Mitsuba stay the references.

- ~~Sponza reference spp~~: settled by P0.9: keep 4096 for both (at 2048 the pbrt-vs-Mitsuba floor rises to 6.2e-3 and even 1e-2 becomes unmeasurable on Sponza).
- V7.4 (partial, scope cut): SVGF on media, whole vs split, vol-cornell-fog 256x256 frame 4: bench/results/v74-media/ (page media-realtime, kernel option frameMedia, chain emissionTemporal); sweeps, moving camera, live page, cloud, docs NOT done.
- V7.5 (done, one seed, frame 4 / 8): `frameMedia` guides now come from a guide ray (first solid surface, medium boundaries skipped; no surface = first boundary entry point; albedo guides 1 through a medium), realtime preset sets `frameMedia` (megakernel, frame, no `volumes.outputs`), the bench session adds `emissionTemporal`. Fog static f4: split relMSE 0.0072 (was 0.021), bias -2.6%, flicker 0.056; svgf-all unchanged 6.6 (it keeps scatter-point guides on purpose); moving f8 split 0.0093 vs raw 1.09. Cloud static f4: split 0.22 / bias -29% vs raw-acc 0.50: the -29% darkening was the chain's fixed far plane (200 m; the WDAS camera is ~1250 from the cloud, SVGF outputs 0 beyond `far`, so only the emission plane survived; every SVGF option read identical to 16 digits). V7.5b: `PipelineSettings.near/far`, the session derives `far` from the scene bounds when they reach past 200 (smaller scenes unchanged). Cloud static f4 now: svgf-split relMSE 0.0125 / bias -3.2%, svgf-all 0.0132 / -2.2%, raw-acc 0.50 (`*-far.pfm`). bench/results/v75-media/.
